# Tech Debt Backlog

Working document for reviewer findings that don't warrant immediate fixes. Appended by `/build`, `/dispatch`, and `/fix-batch` reviewer reports. Operator triages: file Linear stories for items worth doing, delete the rest.

> **Archive:** sections dated before 2026-07-01 (70 sections, 2026-05-03 → 2026-06-30) were moved verbatim to `TECH-DEBT-BACKLOG-archive.md` on 2026-09-01. Append new entries here as before; do not append to the archive.

**Why this exists:** findings used to be auto-filed as Linear `tech-debt:` stories, which created a self-perpetuating queue (reviewer flags items → Lead files story → next batch picks up the story → reviewer flags more items → loop). Findings now land here instead. Nothing in this file should be acted on by an agent without an explicit operator instruction.

## Format for new entries

Append to the bottom under a new dated section. The Lead does this as part of the batch's commits — no separate PR.

```
### 2026-05-03 — fix/batch-2026-05-02 (PR #XXX)

- **[low]** `path/to/file.ts:42` — short description.
  Suggested: one-line fix idea (optional).
- **[med]** `path/other.tsx` — description.
```

Severities: `crit` should never land here (those are auto-fixed during review or sent back to dev). Use `high` / `med` / `low` / `nit`.

## Operator workflow

1. Read new entries when reviewing the PR diff.
2. For each entry: file a Linear story manually, OR delete the entry, OR leave it for later.
3. Pruning is part of triage — old or duplicate items should be removed, not left to bloat.

## Format for skills that parse this file

A future triage skill (or operator-invoked agent) can rely on:

- Each batch is a level-3 heading: `### YYYY-MM-DD — <branch> (PR #<num>)`. Date is ISO. PR ref optional if the PR doesn't exist yet.
- Each finding is a single bullet starting with `- **[<sev>]**` where `<sev>` ∈ {`high`, `med`, `low`, `nit`}.
- File path follows in single backticks; `path:line` is preferred over `path` alone.
- Description is the rest of the bullet up to optional `Suggested:` line indented two spaces.
- The append marker (`<!-- agents append below this line -->`) divides header from entries — skills should read from there forward.

A skill's job is typically: parse → group duplicates by file path → propose Linear stories grouped by area label → present for operator approval. Never auto-file.

### 2026-07-01 — fix/rok-1367-raw-fetch-401-refresh (surfaced during full `vitest run --coverage` gate)

- **[med — pre-existing load flake, NOT this branch; already tracked ROK-1285/ROK-1290]** `web/src/hooks/use-ai-suggestions.test.ts` — one of the real-timer poll-cap tests (`reports pollExhausted=true …` / `resets the poll budget …`, 55s–70s `waitFor` budgets) times out under full-coverage load: `Error: Test timed out in 70000ms` at `Timeout.checkRealTimersCallback` (@testing-library/dom wait-for). Reproduced twice this session (full suite: that file ran 644s; isolated re-run: 1024s for 6 tests — an absurd duration proving CPU/event-loop starvation, not logic). File is UNCHANGED by this branch (`git diff --name-only origin/main..HEAD` does not list it) and this branch's `getAiSuggestions` change is byte-identical for the 200/503 payloads these poll tests use (no 401 → no refresh probe). Passed green when run in a lighter batch earlier the same session. This is the known ROK-1285 (raised `asyncUtilTimeout` for this exact file) / ROK-1290 deep-carrier poll flake — GitHub CI runs sharded + randomized so it lands in a lighter shard and passes. Suggested: convert the two poll-cap tests to fake timers (`vi.useFakeTimers()` + `advanceTimersByTimeAsync`) so the 45s/55s budgets don't depend on wall-clock under load — tracked in ROK-1285/ROK-1290. Not fixing here (out of scope, test not touched by this story).

### 2026-07-02 — fix/rok-1370-poll-start-rescheduling (surfaced during fleet validate-ci)

- **[med]** `api/**` (dozens of files, e.g. `api/src/auth/auth.controller.ts`, `api/src/admin/demo-data-gen-taste-profile.ts`, `api/src/discord-bot/services/discord-embed.factory.ts`) — `npm run prettier:check -w api` fails on a FRESH `npm ci` install: PR #940 (2026-06-30 combined dependency bumps) bumped prettier 3.8.4 → 3.9.3 in the lockfile and 3.9.3 reformats existing files (union-type wrapping etc.). Pre-existing on origin/main: main-repo checkout with pre-#940 node_modules (prettier 3.8.4) passes with 0 warnings on identical file content; the fleet runner passed earlier tonight on cached node_modules and failed after a force-resync triggered fresh `npm ci`. GitHub CI never catches it because the lint job runs `eslint --fix` (silently reformats its own checkout, exits 0) — only `validate-ci.sh`'s `prettier:check` is strict. Blocks the fleet lint gate for every branch validated on a freshly-provisioned runner from now on.
  Suggested: one-shot `npx prettier --write "{src,apps,libs,test}/**/*.ts"` in api (+ web check) as a standalone `chore(format):` PR, immediately after the current in-flight PRs land to minimize rebase conflicts.

### 2026-07-02 — fix/rok-1391-se-lockin-race (surfaced during local Discord-smoke AC gate, overnight batch)

- **[med — pre-existing smoke-suite design, NOT this branch]** `tools/test-bot/src/smoke/` flow category + `/admin/test/{enable,disable}-scheduled-events` — flow tests run at `SMOKE_CONCURRENCY=5` (config.ts:31) against ONE global `scheduledEventsEnabled` toggle; every SE test's `finally` disables it, killing sibling tests' in-flight SE creates (create path reads the toggle at execution). Verbatim log signature: interleaved `POST /admin/test/disable-scheduled-events` + zero `DISCORD | scheduledEvents.create` for the victim's event. Historically masked by the dev reconciler healing missing SEs: when the `ScheduledEventReconciliation_reconcileMissing` cron_jobs row is ABSENT (fresh DB — e.g. CI shards), smoke's `pauseReconciliation` cannot stick (`executeWithTracking` runs untracked, cron-job.service.ts:210-213) and the reconciler heals every ~70s; tonight's dev DB HAS the row → pause sticks → 3-4 SE-dependent flow tests (ROK-944 completion, completion-future, ROK-1347 reconciliation, ROK-1370 pair) fail per parallel run on ANY branch. Confirmed pre-existing: completion-future + 1347-reconciliation PASS serially on this branch. Suggested: per-test SE-toggle scoping (e.g. fixture refcount so disable only lands when no sibling holds enable), or serialize the SE-dependent subset like the `voice` category.
- **[med — pre-existing, NOT this branch]** serial workaround is non-viable: `SMOKE_CONCURRENCY=1 SMOKE_CATEGORY=flow` takes ~50 min → smoke ApiClient JWT expires mid-run (last 4 tests fail `POST … 401`) and Discord rate-limit debt accumulates (guild `scheduledEvents.fetch` 118ms → 807ms → >5s across runs; one undici `Connect Timeout … 162.159.136.232:443`). Suggested: token refresh in the smoke ApiClient (or re-login per test file) before any serialization work.
- **[low — pre-existing, already noted in session notes 2026-07-02]** `Multiple users sign up -> roster updates correctly` — failed all 3 modes tonight (parallel 61s pollForEmbed timeout ×1, serial 82s undici connect-timeout variant ×1; also 2/2 on 2026-07-02 pre-batch). Untouched by this branch. Suggested: next flake pass; likely embed-sync poll budget vs Discord latency, not logic.
- **[high — pre-existing, MAIN-reproduced 2026-07-02, NOT this branch]** reschedule-poll lock-in embed re-render is carried by AMBIENT embed-sync traffic, not the lock-in itself — `POST /scheduling-polls/:id/complete` → UPDATED re-emit correctly recreates the SE (fresh time verified) but produces NO embed re-render: `discord_event_messages.embed_state` stays `rescheduling` until some OTHER event's signup/edit enqueues an embed sync whose level-triggered processor heals it. Deterministic under isolation: `SMOKE_NAME_FILTER="ROK-1370" SMOKE_CONCURRENCY=1` fails `waitForLiveEmbed` on EVERY attempt on BOTH `fix/rok-1391-se-lockin-race` AND `main@89529dfb` (api.log evidence: 1391 branch event 82 4:34:53→4:35:54 stuck; main event 82 5:13:47→5:29:25 stuck, retries included). Masked in CI + parallel local runs by sibling-test embed traffic — which is why #955's smoke passed. User impact: a real lock-in on a quiet server leaves the event card stuck on "RESCHEDULING" until the next unrelated embed touch (or ~15-min reconcile-adjacent traffic). Suggested: make the lock-in path enqueue an explicit embed sync for the linked event (mirror the RESCHEDULING teardown's `updateEmbedState` with a symmetric restore), or have `completeStandalonePoll` enqueue `embed-sync` directly. Candidate Linear story drafted in the 2026-07-03 morning report; operator files.

### 2026-07-06 — dispatch batch ROK-1365 + ROK-1392 (surfaced during dispatch)

- **[med — pre-existing CI coverage gap, NOT this branch]** The GitHub `discord-smoke` job's path filter (`.github/workflows/*` Discord-smoke `paths:` set — discord-bot/**, notifications/**, events/signups*, events/event-lifecycle*, admin/demo-test*, tools/test-bot/src/smoke/**) and the mirrored local detector in `scripts/validate-ci.sh` do NOT include `api/src/lineups/standalone-poll/**`. But standalone-poll enqueues an embed-sync at reschedule-poll lock-in (ROK-1392) → it directly drives Discord embed behavior. Net: an embed-affecting change under `lineups/standalone-poll/**` gets its GitHub `discord-smoke` job path-filtered to SKIPPED, so GitHub provides NO Discord behavioral coverage for it. ROK-1392 was safe because the companion-bot smoke was run LOCALLY (GREEN ×2, the `ROK-1370: lock-in restores the live embed` acceptance test), but a future embed-affecting lineup change could merge with zero Discord CI signal. Suggested: add `api/src/lineups/standalone-poll/**` (and audit sibling lineup embed paths) to both the GitHub discord-smoke `paths:` filter and the `validate-ci.sh` Discord-smoke trigger list, so embed-affecting lineup changes auto-trigger the Discord smoke on push.

GitHub CI runs integration SHARDED so it never hits the monolithic single-process accumulation. - **[low — pre-existing local-env, NOT this branch]** `./scripts/validate-migrations.sh` fails locally at migration `0000` with `psql: connection to server on socket "/var/run/postgresql/.s.PGSQL.5432" failed: FATAL: database "raid_ledger" does not exist` — the script's psql hits the laptop's default local socket (PGHOST/PGDATABASE from the shell profile) instead of the temp container's TCP port. Passes cleanly on the fleet runner (`Applied 153 migration(s) cleanly`). Local-shell-env artifact, not a script/migration bug. Suggested: `validate-migrations.sh` should `unset PGHOST PGDATABASE PGPORT` (or set them explicitly to the temp container) before its psql loop so a developer's ambient PG env can't hijack the connection.
- **[note]** The prettier 3.9.3 vs 3.8.4 (#940) drift documented 2026-07-02 recurred on this branch's fresh-`npm install` worktree (local `prettier:check` flags ~20 pre-existing files; api-workspace prettier is the CI-authoritative context). ROK-313's own api files were formatted to 3.9.3 in-branch. No new action — the standing `chore(format):` suggestion stands.

### 2026-07-13 — fix/scheduling-poll-voter-membership (surfaced during adversarial review of the voter-membership fix)

- **[low]** `api/src/lineups/scheduling/scheduling.controller.ts:152` — `DELETE .../votes` (retractAllVotes) and the poll-page GET remain ungated for private lineups after the vote/suggest surface gained `assertCallerMayVote` (fixed in this same branch — Codex security P2). Retract only deletes the caller's own votes (self-cleanup, harmless) and the GET is read-only, but for full parity with nominate/game-vote the whole private-lineup scheduling surface could run the participation check. Suggested: add `assertCallerMayVote` to retract + consider gating `getSchedulePoll` for private lineups.
- **[low]** `api/src/lineups/lineups-bandwagon.helpers.ts:71-91` `checkAutoPromote` — schedule-vote enrollment grows the member set of `suggested` matches but never triggers the promote check itself; the NEXT bandwagon join computes its threshold percentage against the enlarged member count and can promote earlier than the pre-fix math would have. Behaviorally defensible (slot votes are a demand signal) but undocumented. Suggested: either call the promote check after vote-enrollment on `suggested` matches too (consistent), or note the semantic in the helper docstring.
- **[low]** `api/src/lineups/standalone-poll/standalone-poll-reminder.service.ts:159-173` + `lineup-reminder-target.helpers.ts` — sticky vote-enrollment means a user who un-votes (or retracts all votes) stays a match member and becomes a standing "you haven't voted" reminder-cron target with no leave-poll mechanism. Matches from-match semantics (member sets never shrink) but is new for standalone polls. Suggested: consider excluding users whose LAST action was retract-all, or add an explicit leave/mute affordance if operators report DM complaints.

### 2026-07-15 — fix/running-late-followups (surfaced during high-effort code review of the running-late/delay follow-ups)

- **[low — defense in depth]** `formatEpoch` (`api/src/notifications/format-helpers.ts:19-29`) renders ANY residual `<t:` token that reaches `buildPlaintextContent` in the server process TZ (no `timeZone` option), and `stripDiscordMarkup` (:39) discards the style char so `:R` (relative "in N hours") collapses to an absolute duplicate. Even after the two fixes above, any future notification type that stores `<t:>` markup inherits the bug. Suggested: thread the recipient timezone into the DM job data and have `formatEpoch` render in it (and render `:R` as a real relative delta). Note: `tools/test-bot/src/helpers/discord-markup.ts` mirrors `format-helpers.ts` and must stay in sync.
- **[low — product/observability]** The new `running_late` notification type is default-on across inApp/push/discord and has no toggle row in the web `NotificationPreferencesSection` (`web/src/components/profile/NotificationPreferencesSection.tsx`) — same as the shipped `event_delayed` precedent, so users cannot opt out of either. It also sets no `expiresAt`, so a late notice lingers in the in-app list after the event. Suggested (if operators report noise): add prefs rows for `event_delayed` + `running_late` and set `expiresAt = event end` on both.

### 2026-07-17 — fix/batch-2026-07-17 (PR pending)

- **[RESOLVED, was med]** `.github/workflows/discord-smoke.yml` + `scripts/validate-ci.sh:305` discord-smoke filter gap for `api/src/lineups/standalone-poll/**` (2026-07-06 entry) — both filter locations extended in this batch; CLAUDE.md trigger lists updated to match.

Actual mechanism: `pg_isready` succeeds against initdb's temporary socket-only bootstrap server before `POSTGRES_DB` exists. Fixed in this batch: readiness poll now runs `SELECT 1` against `raid_ledger` over TCP (`-h 127.0.0.1`) — the bootstrap server is socket-only, so TCP success guarantees the final server.

The fleet lockfile-exact-refresh guard half (ROK-1404 AC3) remains open; this batch hit it live (fleet tasks 88dd18de5e4c/0c0e69d49eff: 42 phantom prettier errors until `npm ci` on both ends).

Violates the ROK-1362 every-blocking-wait-caps-at-120s design — spin appears to run its build/deploy synchronously inside the tool handler instead of dispatching a task like `rl_env_deploy` does. Bit this batch's Chrome-MCP gate (fell back to local deploy_dev). ROK-1338 no-SSH umbrella adjacent.
  Suggested: make `rl_env_spin` async-by-default returning `{task_id}` (laptop or VM task), polled via `rl_task_status` like deploy/clone-prod.

### 2026-07-18 — fix/batch-2026-07-17-b (PR #994)

- **[RESOLVED, was med]** `tools/mcp-rl-fleet/src/tools/__tests__/test-plan-v2.spec.ts` "ROK-1337 AC2 — executeWait watches the slug directory" red (2026-06-08 entry, ~L584) — **diagnosis correction:** neither an fs.watch mock wiring issue nor a path-normalization regression (the product code watches the correct slug directory). Actual mechanism: `vi.resetModules()` in `beforeEach` wipes the `resolveProxmoxHost` memo, so every test re-paid a REAL `dns.promises.lookup('rl-infra.lan')` inside the AC2 test's 100ms fixed race — runners with >100ms DNS never captured the spawn argv. Fixed in this batch: non-default `RL_PROXMOX_HOST` stub (RFC-5737 address, short-circuits before DNS) + `vi.waitFor` on the capture instead of a fixed timer. 15 consecutive green runs; suite 2.16s → 200ms.

Green against the live dev env (13 passed) and inside the full 646-test suite run.

### 2026-07-24 — rok-1418-suppression-scoping (surfaced during B1)

Deferred findings from the ROK-1418 suppression-scoping + bounded-extension fix (D4). All are out of scope for this `--static` PR (spec Deliverable 8); recorded here so B3/B4/B5 and the operator triage don't rediscover them. None is a failure on `origin/main` — they are known asymmetries the D4 change touches but deliberately does not resolve.

- **[RESOLVED 2026-09-06 in ROK-1456, was med]** Hoisted into `AdHocEventService.ensureNotSuppressed` + the single-use `SpawnClearance` receipt (`api/src/discord-bot/services/ad-hoc-spawn-clearance.ts`) that `spawnNewEvent` now requires, so the guard runs exactly once per spawn-path join; the "twice on every game-binding join" framing below was overstated (see the 2026-09-01 ROK-1445 entry for the measured per-path counts). Original entry: Duplicate suppression check per join — `api/src/discord-bot/listeners/voice-state-join.handlers.ts:77` (`handleGameBindingJoin`) calls `adHocEventService.trySuppressForScheduled(...)` and, when it returns false, `checkGameBindingThreshold` → `handleVoiceJoin` (`api/src/discord-bot/services/ad-hoc-event.service.ts:105`) runs the same suppression query a second time. Both queries + the (now bounded) `extended_until` write execute twice on every game-binding join. Not deleted here: the listener call is B3/B4 blast radius (ROK-1415 rewrites `checkGameBindingThreshold`). Suggested: after B3/B4 land, have the listener pass its suppression result through to `handleVoiceJoin` (or drop the listener-side check) so the query runs once.
- **[med]** Suppression-window extension skips the sync fan-out that the scheduled auto-extend path performs. `extendScheduledEventWindow` (`api/src/discord-bot/services/ad-hoc-suppression.helpers.ts`) writes only `extended_until` + `updated_at`, whereas `event-auto-extend.service.ts:161-180` also `eventCache.invalidate()`/`refresh()`, `adHocGateway.emitEndTimeExtended()`, and `scheduledEventService.updateEndTime()` (Discord scheduled-event end sync). A suppressed-join extension therefore leaves the event cache, the live web clients, and the Discord event end time stale until the next natural refresh. Suggested: extract the auto-extend fan-out into a shared helper and call it from the suppression write, or emit `emitEndTimeExtended` from the suppression path at minimum.
- **[low]** Dead OR-terms retained in the suppression clause. `buildBindingClause` still carries `events.channel_binding_id = <bindingId>` and (for the game path) the sibling subquery, but for `is_ad_hoc = false` rows `events.channel_binding_id` is only ever written by the ad-hoc INSERT, so both channel-based terms are structurally unreachable for scheduled events. Kept byte-identical on purpose (the intended landing zone if scheduled events ever gain a binding link — see spec D4(a)), not deleted. Suggested: leave until scheduled events gain a `channel_binding_id`, then either wire it or delete the terms with a test.
- **[low]** `extractGameIds` collapses to "all games" on any null-game or non-monitor binding. `api/src/discord-bot/services/voice-attendance-flush.helpers.ts:260-268` returns `null` (meaning "count every member regardless of game") as soon as one binding on the channel is not `game-voice-monitor` or has `gameId === null`. On a mixed channel (e.g. a monitor + a general-lobby, the permitted B2 shape) this silently widens attendance/flush counting to all games. Orthogonal to D4's suppression fix but adjacent in the same multi-binding topology. Suggested: decide whether a null-game monitor should widen the whole channel or be skipped, and encode it explicitly.
- **[low]** Multi-binding runtime asymmetries (batch plan flagged as deferred — the "§8" reference in spec D4 Deliverable 8 points at the resolved-questions section; the enumeration lives in the batch synthesis). Concrete instances the D4 change exposes but does not fix: (a) the anchored game clause scopes by the *querying* channelId while the sibling subquery matches *any* sibling binding on the physical channel, so game-match and sibling-match use different channel semantics; (b) `matchedBy` is a best-effort SQL `CASE` and can mislabel when more than one OR-term matches; (c) suppression runs once per binding, so an N-monitor channel evaluates the same scheduled event N times (the SQL monotonic guard dedups only the *write*, not the reads/logs). Suggested: revisit as a group when the multi-binding admin work (ROK-1416/B5) settles the intended per-channel topology.
- **[low]** `api/src/discord-bot/services/ad-hoc-suppression.helpers.ts:157` — `skipCappedWarnedAt` throttle Map never prunes expired TTL entries, so distinct capped event ids accumulate for the process lifetime (Codex review finding, ROK-1418 PR). In practice bounded by the daily 5AM Watchtower restart, so impact is a day's worth of capped events (tiny). Suggested: prune entries older than the 30-min TTL on each `warnSkipCapped` call, mirroring the prune-on-call pattern D5 Tier 1 mandates for the ROK-1417 trace Maps.

### 2026-07-24 — rok-1419-nonseries-unique-index (PR pending)

- **[med]** `api/src/drizzle/schema/channel-bindings.ts` (index `channel_bindings_nonseries_nullgame_unique`) + `api/src/igdb/igdb-dedup-fk-reassign.helpers.ts:167-186` — the new partial unique indexes create a `games` DELETE collision hazard. ROK-842 permits two `game-voice-monitor` bindings on ONE channel for DIFFERENT games (both `game_id` non-null → distinct rows in the game-partial index). A `DELETE FROM games` that removes BOTH those games fires the FK `ON DELETE set null`, collapsing both bindings to `(guild, channel, 'game-voice-monitor', NULL)` — which now BOTH qualify for the null-game partial index, so the second SET NULL raises 23505 and aborts the whole DELETE. Pre-index this succeeded. `safeReassign`'s bare `catch { ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT }` swallows it silently, so an IGDB dedup that merges two such twin-bound games would no-op that group with no signal. Narrow trigger (needs 2+ same-channel same-purpose monitors for different games AND both games deleted together), hence med not high.
  Suggested: before `DELETE FROM games`, null-and-dedupe co-channel monitor bindings first, or teach `safeReassign` to detect 23505 on `channel_bindings_nonseries_*` and repoint the surviving row instead of swallowing.
- **[RESOLVED in-batch, was low]** `api/src/drizzle/migrations/0161_channel_bindings_nonseries_unique.sql` (DML statements 3-4) — the audit `moved_events` / `events_repointed` / `events_nulled` columns were recomputed UNCONDITIONALLY by `run_id` on every execution, so a `reconcile-migrations.mjs` re-run after the losers were deleted would wipe the operator UNDO record to empty/0. Fixed pre-merge by the Lead (this PR): both audit UPDATEs now carry `EXISTS` re-run guards (statement 3: only rows with entries in this run's `moved` set — first-run empties keep the column DEFAULT `'[]'`; statement 4: only while the loser row still exists, i.e. pre-DELETE). Validated: `validate-migrations.sh` 162 clean + the B2-2/B2-3 dedupe/restore suites 20/20 against the edited file.
- **[low]** `api/tsconfig.json` (local only) — bare `npx tsc --noEmit -p api/tsconfig.json` reports ~36k `TS2304/TS2593 Cannot find name 'it'/'expect'/'describe'` across EVERY `*.spec.ts` locally (both this worktree and the main checkout, unaffected by `npm install`). `@types/jest` is present at the repo-root `node_modules/@types` but tsc's automatic `@types` discovery is not picking it up for the spec files; jest itself (ts-jest) compiles them fine and GitHub CI's fresh `npm ci` typechecks green (main merges), so this is a local `@types` auto-discovery quirk, not a code failure. It makes `validate-ci.sh --static`'s typecheck step unusable as a local signal — production source typechecks clean and `nest build` passes.
  Suggested: add an explicit `"types": ["node", "jest"]` (or `typeRoots`) to `api/tsconfig.json`, or a `tsconfig.spec.json`, so the local typecheck matches CI.
  **VERIFIED RESOLVED 2026-09-01** — measured on a clean main checkout: `npx tsc --noEmit -p api/tsconfig.json` exits **0** with **zero** errors (no TS2304/TS2593 jest-globals noise, and none of the 156 residual spec errors). Fixed at some point after 2026-08-21; no `types` array was needed. Do NOT re-file or 'fix' this — the local typecheck is a trustworthy signal today.

### 2026-07-25 — rok-1415-binding-invariant-guard (surfaced during B3)

Deferred by design in the ROK-1415 root fix (spec D2(f)): the invariant guard enforces WRITE intent at the two service sites only. The following paths can still produce the inert `(voice, game-voice-monitor, NULL)` shape and are deliberately NOT prevented — they are exactly why the runtime tolerance (raw-count spawn) + boot detection (`[binding-heal] INERT` WARN + Sentry) + `GET /admin/discord/bindings/health` ship in the same PR:

- **[med]** `pg_restore` / prod-clone flows — `channel_bindings` is not excluded from backups, so restoring a dump that predates a game deletion resurrects inert rows wholesale. Detection fires on next bot CONNECT; the ROK-1416 UI repair (B5) is the remediation path. Suggested: none (tolerated by design; revisit only if inert-row Sentry events become recurrent).
- **[med]** IGDB dedup raw reassign (`igdb-dedup-fk-reassign.helpers.ts` `safeReassign`) and 0140-style bulk UPDATE migrations — raw SQL bypasses the service guard AND `normalizeAndDeleteGames`. The dedup helpers currently repoint bindings before deleting games (verified — they never orphan on their own), but nothing structurally forces future raw paths to. Suggested: route any future app-side games deletion through `normalizeAndDeleteGames` (it owns delete + collision-aware normalization).
- **[low]** `voice-attendance-flush.helpers.ts::extractGameIds` still widens to all-games when a null-game binding is present on a mixed channel (pre-existing, flagged in B1's section) — the ROK-1415 tolerance makes null-game monitors SPAWN now, so this asymmetry is reachable slightly more often. Same suggested fix as B1's entry.
- **[med]** `channel-bindings-invariant.helpers.ts::normalizeAndDeleteGames` has NO production call site yet (Codex ROK-1415 review P1) — the only current app-side game deletes are the IGDB dedup paths, which deliberately repoint bindings to the canonical game (a DIFFERENT policy than normalize-to-lobby) before deleting, so wiring them through the normalizer would change dedup semantics. The helper is the sanctioned entry point for any FUTURE app-side delete (admin endpoint, cleanup job). B2's games-DELETE 23505/safeReassign entry remains the tracker for the dedup-side hazard; the normalizer is the building block for its fix. Suggested: route any new games-delete path through the helper; revisit safeReassign with it when the dedup hazard is picked up.
- **[low]** `voice-state.handlers.ts:211` `!binding.gameId` treats `gameId === 0` as missing (Codex ROK-1415 security P3) — the contract now correctly derives a monitor for game id 0, but a monitor bound to a hypothetical id-0 game would zero-count at getGameFilteredCount and NOT be flagged inert (detection checks `=== null`). Unreachable today (serial ids start at 1; `gameExists(0)` 404s the POST path) and the line is deliberately untouched in B3 (ROK-1390/1394 degrade-guard adjacency, plan D2(g)/D5). Suggested: change to `binding.gameId == null` in a batch that owns the degrade-guard tests.

### 2026-08-15 — combined/unblock-2026-08-15 (surfaced during /unblock-prs dependabot batch validation)

- **[med]** `npm run test:integration -w api` (`api/jest.integration.config.js`, `--runInBand`) dies with `FATAL ERROR: Ineffective mark-compacts near heap limit Allocation failed - JavaScript heap out of memory` → `Abort trap: 6` (exit 134). Reproduced TWICE: once at Node's default heap, once with the previously-documented workaround `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192` — **8GB is no longer sufficient**, which is a regression against the workaround recorded in `reference_bullmq_ioredis_test_carrier`. Failure point: 74 suites PASS, then `src/events/signups-embed-sync.integration.spec.ts` is reported `FAIL (1002.482 s)` as the OOM victim. That spec is only the carrier, not the cause — run in isolation it passes 8/8 in 6.0s. Jest also prints `Force exiting Jest: Have you considered using --detectOpenHandles` at the end of the isolated run, i.e. the open-handle/socket leak (BullMQ ioredis family) is still live and accumulates across the serial run until heap is exhausted.
  Pre-existing / NOT caused by this branch: the branch is a pure dependabot dependency batch, and `git diff origin/main..HEAD -- api/package.json` touches **only `devDependencies`** (`@types/node`, `eslint`, `globals`, `prettier`, `ts-jest`, `typescript-eslint`) — zero api RUNTIME dependencies changed, so api server behaviour under test is byte-identical to main. The only bump that touches test execution at all is `ts-jest` 29.4.11→29.4.12 (patch), and 74 consecutive passing suites plus the isolated carrier pass demonstrate transpilation is healthy. Invisible in GitHub CI because the integration job is sharded (no single process accumulates the whole suite) AND because this diff is deps-only, so GitHub's path filter skips `api`/`web` jobs entirely.
  Suggested: (a) short term, raise the documented local workaround above 8GB (12–16GB) and update `reference_bullmq_ioredis_test_carrier`, or shard/`--maxWorkers` the local integration run the way CI does; (b) real fix, run the suite with `--detectOpenHandles` to identify the leaking BullMQ/ioredis handles and close them in `afterAll` teardown so heap does not grow monotonically across suites.

### 2026-08-19 — combined/unblock-2026-08-16 (surfaced during /unblock-prs dependabot batch validation)

- **[high]** `npm run test:integration -w api` is **currently unusable on the local machine** — and this is now demonstrated pre-existing by a clean-baseline run, not inferred. Two distinct terminal signatures observed in one session on the same env:
  1. On `combined/unblock-2026-08-16`: `Segmentation fault: 11` (native crash, no OOM text anywhere in the log) after **64 suites passed**.
  2. On a clean `origin/main@64b30940` checkout (main worktree, untouched tree, node_modules matching main's lockfile): **13 suites FAIL**, all by timeout, with per-suite wall times of **190s–1086s**. No segfault and no OOM in the baseline — the run simply crawls until suites blow their Jest timeout.

  Baseline failures on clean main: `availability.integration.spec.ts` (914s), `cron-jobs/cron-job` (996s), `discord-bot/ad-hoc-suppression` (925s), `discord-bot/services/scheduled-event.capacity-recovery` (1087s), `drizzle/schema/community-lineup-matches` (514s), `igdb/igdb-discover.community-playing` (931s), `lineups/common-ground-taste` (961s), `lineups/lineup-deadline-transition-notify` (192s), `lineups/lineup-notification-tiebreaker` (996s), `lineups/standalone-poll/standalone-poll-reschedule-cycle` (959s), `lineups/submit/lineup-submit` (921s), `notifications/post-event-followup` (956s), `users/users.integration.spec.ts` (327s).

  **Pre-existing / NOT caused by the dependabot batch.** The discriminating experiment was run explicitly: the same command on a clean `origin/main` tree with none of the branch's changes fails 13 suites. The branch's diff touches only `devDependencies` (`@nestjs/testing` 11.1.28→11.1.29, `@types/node`, `eslint`, `globals`, `typescript-eslint`, `@testing-library/*`, `eslint-plugin-react-refresh`, `tsx`) — zero api runtime dependencies. Note the varying signature (heap OOM on 2026-08-15, segfault + timeouts here) is itself evidence of environmental instability rather than a deterministic code fault.

  **This supersedes nothing in the 2026-08-15 entry** — that entry recorded the heap-OOM variant and correctly noted 8GB was insufficient; this entry adds the clean-main baseline that the earlier one lacked, plus the segfault and timeout variants.

  Invisible in GitHub CI for two independent reasons: the integration job is sharded (no single process accumulates the whole serial suite), AND deps-only diffs are skipped by GitHub's `api`/`web` path filter entirely.

  Suggested: (a) treat local `--full` integration results as **unreliable on this host** until fixed — do not gate deps-only PRs on them; (b) investigate host-level contention first (Docker Desktop `raid-ledger-db` had `Up 36 hours` uptime during these runs; a container bounce + `docker system df` check is the cheap first probe); (c) run with `--detectOpenHandles` to chase the BullMQ/ioredis handle leak already recorded in `reference_bullmq_ioredis_test_carrier`; (d) shard the local integration run the way CI does (`--maxWorkers`) so no single process accumulates the whole suite.

### 2026-08-19 — rok-1398-coop-detail-section (surfaced during ROK-1398 TDD test authoring)

- **[med]** `scripts/smoke/game-detail.smoke.spec.ts:16` — `navigateToFirstGame` soft-skips the WHOLE spec on the local dev env: `npx playwright test scripts/smoke/game-detail.smoke.spec.ts` reports `16 skipped` on both projects, because no `a[href*="/games/"]` becomes visible on `/games` within the 10s probe. The DB is not empty — `GET /games/search?q=a` returns seeded games (ARK, etc.) — so the skip reason it prints (`No games seeded — skipping game detail tests`) is misleading. Pre-existing: `navigateToFirstGame` is untouched by this branch (the branch only appends a new `describe` block that navigates to `/games/:id` directly), and the env is a plain `deploy_dev.sh` dev stack. Net effect: every existing game-detail smoke assertion is silently inert locally, so local Playwright runs give false confidence for game-detail UI work.
  Suggested: replace the soft `test.skip` with an explicit precondition (query `/games/search` via `api-helpers`, hard-fail if games exist but no card link renders), and investigate why `/games` renders no card links locally (discover-page gating / lineup banner overlay / onboarding modal are the likely candidates).

### 2026-08-21 — rok-1401-coop-fit-signals (surfaced during ROK-1401 `validate-ci.sh --full`)

- ~~**[high]** `scripts/validate-ci.sh:250-252` (+ `run_typecheck` at `:418-426`) — **a real TypeScript failure is reported as `SKIPPED`, not `FAIL`, and the run continues.** `run_step` uses `rc == 2` as the SKIPPED sentinel (`elif [ "$rc" -eq 2 ]; then ... record_result "$name" "SKIPPED"`), but `tsc` exits with **2** when diagnostics are present (verified on this host: `npx tsc --noEmit <file-with-TS2322>; echo $?` → `2`). The ROK-1336 #5 fix (`npx tsc --noEmit -p api/tsconfig.json || return $?`) faithfully propagates tsc's rc, which then collides with the sentinel. Observed live: my `--full` run printed `TypeScript (all): SKIPPED` while `npx tsc --noEmit -p api/tsconfig.json` on the same tree emitted 3× `TS2345` in `api/src/lineups/lineups-match-response.helpers.spec.ts`. Pre-existing — `scripts/validate-ci.sh` is untouched by this branch, and the collision is structural (any tsc error yields rc 2). Note the backlog's 2026-07 audit line ~609 marks an *earlier* `run_typecheck` masking bug as fixed; this is the successor bug that fix left behind. Only GitHub CI catches these today.~~ **FIXED 2026-08-30** — `run_step` no longer infers a skip from an exit code. A step now declares one explicitly via `skip_step` AND must exit cleanly, so a tool returning 2 for a real failure (`tsc`, `tsc -b`) can no longer be reported as SKIPPED. Regression test: `scripts/test/validate-ci-skip-contract.test.sh` (verified non-vacuous — 5 cases fail against the pre-fix script).
  Suggested: stop overloading the exit code — have skip-capable steps `return 200` (or set a `STEP_SKIPPED=1` global) instead of `2`, and/or normalize in `run_typecheck` with `|| return 1`.

### 2026-08-21 — rok-1401-coop-fit-signals (surfaced during ROK-1401 local verification)

- **[med]** `web/src/hooks/use-ai-suggestions.test.ts` (ROK-1316 pending→poll cases) — real-timer polling tests (15 × 3s budgets, 70s per-test timeout) fail non-deterministically whenever the host sleeps mid-run: observed 2-of-6 failing with per-test durations of 394s–996s on BOTH this branch AND a clean `origin/main` (0c380c29) checkout; `pmset -g log` shows the laptop cycling Sleep/DarkWake every ~16 min overnight. Pre-existing — file untouched by this branch; varying failing subset between runs. Same mechanism also produced the 9-hour "Playwright FAIL" on review/coop-batch-1 (2026-08-20) and killed two dev subagents mid-response.
  Suggested: (a) fake timers (`vi.useFakeTimers` + `advanceTimersByTime`) for the poll-budget cases so wall-clock sleep can't skew them; (b) `scripts/validate-ci.sh` and `/build` long-running steps should wrap under `caffeinate -is` (process-level sleep assertion) so local gates don't silently degrade on a closed-lid laptop.

### 2026-08-22 — rok-1401-coop-fit-signals (surfaced during ROK-1401 ship gate)

- **[med]** `scripts/validate-ci.sh` integration step — **an integration OOM is reported as a plain `FAIL` with no indication of how much of the suite never ran.** On this branch's `--full` run, jest died at `FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit` / `Abort trap: 6` after **75 of 134** integration specs; the other **59 never executed** — including BOTH specs this branch adds (`api/src/lineups/common-ground-coop.integration.spec.ts`, `api/src/lineups/lineups-coop-fit.integration.spec.ts`) plus `common-ground.integration.spec.ts`, `lineups-matches.integration.spec.ts`, `cooptimus-sync.integration.spec.ts` and `cooptimus-prose-gating.integration.spec.ts`. The log's long run of `PASS` lines plus a well-documented OOM (entry 2026-08-16, line ~827) makes "known flake, everything relevant passed" the natural read — and it is wrong. The OOM itself is already tracked; this bullet is about the **reporting**, which is what makes it dangerous. All 59 were re-run here in batches of 14 and pass (6 co-op specs: 96 tests; remainder green), so nothing is failing — the gap was purely invisible coverage loss.
  Suggested: on a non-zero integration exit, have `validate-ci.sh` diff `PASS`/`FAIL` spec paths in the jest output against `find api/src -name '*.integration.spec.ts'` and print `N spec(s) never ran:` with the list, so the summary can never imply coverage it did not achieve. Cheap follow-on: batch the local invocation (~14 specs per process) instead of one `--runInBand` process, which sidesteps the OOM entirely — raising `--max-old-space-size` does not (8GB already documented insufficient).
- **[nit]** `scripts/smoke/lineup-nominating-composite.smoke.spec.ts` (pre-existing, from #924) — `await page.waitForTimeout(500)` violates the STRICT "no `sleep()` in smoke tests" rule. Untouched by this branch (only the trailing `void apiGet` suppression was corrected here, since ROK-1401's test now genuinely uses that import).
  Suggested: replace with the deterministic wait the surrounding assertions already use (`expect(...).toBeVisible({ timeout })` / `pollForCondition`).

### 2026-08-23 — fix/poll-nudge-24h-cadence (surfaced during poll-nudge cadence verification)

- **[med]** `api/src/lineups/standalone-poll/standalone-poll-reschedule-cycle.integration.spec.ts` — **cold-start schema race: the first integration run in a session can fail 18 tests with migration DDL errors rather than assertion failures.** Observed once on this branch: the run printed a wall of `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT ... FOREIGN KEY` errors inside `PL/pgSQL function inline_code_block` (community_lineup_schedule_slots / _votes / _match_members / community_lineup_entries FKs) and reported `18 failed, 25 passed`. Not caused by the branch — the diff touches only two nudge constants, a poll-duration default and doc comments, none of which participate in schema setup. Did not reproduce in **5 consecutive subsequent runs** (1 spec-alone + 4 three-spec combos, 43/43 each), and the same three-spec combo passed 42/42 on a stashed clean `origin/main` tree. Signature is distinct from the documented BullMQ/ioredis socket carrier and the integration-404 carrier: this one fails during test-app schema bootstrap, before any request is made.
  Suggested: have the integration `test-app` bootstrap take an advisory lock (or serialize schema setup across Jest workers) so two workers can't race the same `CREATE`/`ALTER` DDL against the shared test DB; failing that, log a clear "schema bootstrap failed — re-run" banner so the next agent doesn't spend a cycle bisecting their own diff.
- ~~**[med]** `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md` Step 8.5 — **the Codex pre-push review command is invalid on codex-cli 0.133.0 and silently degrades the gate.** The skill runs `codex review --base main "<critical-only prompt>"`, but 0.133.0 rejects it: `error: the argument '--base <BRANCH>' cannot be used with '[PROMPT]'` — the two are mutually exclusive (`Usage: codex review --base <BRANCH> [PROMPT]` notwithstanding). Falling back to a bare `codex review --base main` then hit a second, independent failure: `ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to refresh available models: ... unknown variant \`max\`, expected one of \`none\`, \`minimal\`, \`low\`, \`medium\`, \`high\`, \`xhigh\`` — the installed CLI can't decode the server's model list, so it emitted ~2800 lines of file dump and no verdict. Net effect: the "one review pass" that the trivial-tier fast lane treats as non-negotiable produced nothing, and the skill's own error path ("log and proceed") means this failure is easy to wave through unnoticed on every push.~~
  Suggested: (a) drop the custom prompt when `--base` is used, or move the critical-only instruction into `~/.codex/config.toml`; (b) upgrade codex-cli past the `reasoning_level: max` variant; (c) have Step 8.5 distinguish "Codex ran and found nothing" from "Codex never ran" in the Step 12 report table, so a silently-skipped review can't read as a passed one. **FIXED 2026-10-02** — (a) the custom prompt was dropped (`codex review --base main` alone) in the 2026-08-28 config ride-along on #1055; (b) codex-cli 0.152.1 is installed, past the `max` variant; (c) `/push` Step 8.5 now records `not-run (<reason>)` / `ran-no-verdict (exit <rc>)` / `verdict: …`, and the Step 12 report has a `Codex review` row that is never blank.

### 2026-08-23 — fix/cooptimus-combo-from-game-page (surfaced during the Co-Optimus combo ship gate)

- **[med]** `scripts/smoke/admin-user-moderation.smoke.spec.ts:50` — **"kicks a member (Kicked badge appears) then unkicks (badge clears)" is flaky under parallel workers, on BOTH desktop and mobile.** Measured across three full local runs on this branch: run 1 it FAILED on mobile; run 2 it passed while two unrelated specs failed instead; a targeted re-run of the three suspect specs at `--workers=2` produced `41 passed, 2 flaky` with this test flaky on both projects. It passes deterministically in isolation (2/2). No deterministic failure survived isolation in any run, and the branch's diff (Co-Optimus sync + game-detail co-op section) has no path into the kick/unkick flow. Strong prior on the cause: moderation actions revoke the cached admin token shared across smoke workers, so whichever worker holds it next fails — the same interaction already recorded for `blockUser`. This is NOT the documented community-lineup/onboarding flake set; that set also appeared in these runs, separately.
  Suggested: give the moderation specs their own admin token (or re-authenticate after any kick/unkick/ban) instead of sharing the cached one, so a moderation action cannot invalidate a sibling worker's session mid-run.
- **[med]** `scripts/deploy_dev.sh --ci --rebuild` — **`--rebuild` did not refresh `packages/contract/dist/`.** After running it on this branch, `dist/games.schema.d.ts` was still June-dated and did not contain the field this branch adds to the contract, while source, `tsc --noEmit`, and every unit suite were green (they resolve the contract differently than the running servers do). The API and Vite therefore booted against a stale contract, and an e2e run against that env would have "passed" while exercising code that lacked the change. An explicit `npm run build -w @raid-ledger/contract` fixed it; the servers then needed a restart, since Nest's watcher does not watch `node_modules`.
  Suggested: have `--rebuild` actually rebuild the contract workspace (and fail loudly if the emitted `dist` is older than `packages/contract/src`), so a stale `dist` cannot silently invalidate local e2e results.
- **[nit]** Vite binds **IPv6-only** (`[::1]:5173`), so `curl 127.0.0.1:5173` fails while `localhost:5173` succeeds. Any readiness probe using the IPv4 literal reports the web server down when it is healthy — in `validate-ci.sh` that surfaces as `Playwright: SKIPPED — Dev env not responding`, i.e. silently skipped e2e rather than an error.
  Suggested: probe `localhost` (or try both families) in the e2e gate's readiness check.
### 2026-08-23 — fix/batch-2026-08-22 (surfaced during per-story reviewer passes on ROK-1424/1425/1427)

- **[RESOLVED 2026-08-24 — PR pending, branch `fix/emit-connected-isolation`]** ~~**[med]**~~ `api/src/discord-bot/discord-bot-client.service.ts:329-341` — `emitConnected()` wraps
  `emitAsync` in ONE try/catch around the whole chain rather than per-listener. EventEmitter2 invokes
  listeners in a plain loop, so a synchronous throw from any earlier `CONNECTED` subscriber aborts the
  rest and every listener registered after it silently never attaches. Only trace is
  `Error in CONNECTED event handlers`. This is an unclosed candidate root cause for ROK-1425 and is
  NOT fixed by that story's commits.
  Suggested: wrap each subscriber, or emit per-listener with individual catch + log.
  **Fixed:** `emitConnected` now delegates to `emitIsolated` (`discord-bot-event-dispatch.helpers.ts`), which enumerates subscribers and runs each in its own `Promise.allSettled` slot. Premise verified empirically against a real EventEmitter2 (old: A,B then abort; new: A,B,C,D). Regression spec falsified — 8 of 11 cases fail against the pre-fix implementation.
- **[med]** `api/src/discord-bot/listeners/signup-interaction.listener.ts:103`,
  `api/src/discord-bot/listeners/pug-invite.listener.ts:89` — null their handler refs on DISCONNECTED
  without calling `removeListener`. Functionally correct TODAY only because `connect()` always builds a
  new `Client`; if the client service ever reuses a Client across DISCONNECTED/CONNECTED these stack
  instantly. Half-migrated pattern now that eight siblings use `DiscordListenerBinding`.
  Suggested: convert both to `DiscordListenerBinding` for uniformity.
- **[low]** `api/src/discord-bot/listeners/discord-listener-binding.ts:19-24` — `GatewayBinding` is
  exported with `handler: (...args: never[]) => void`. Every function is assignable to that, so the
  `gatewayBinding()` factory can be bypassed with a wrong handler signature that still typechecks.
  Suggested: brand the type, or don't export it and type `attach` as `ReturnType<typeof gatewayBinding>[]`.
- **[nit]** `api/src/discord-bot/listeners/reschedule-response.listener.ts` — 298/300 eslint-counted
  lines. Two lines of headroom before `max-lines` hard-fails CI. Next author must extract first.

  `fetchLateGraceByUserId` filters only on `eventId` + `runningLateAt IS NOT NULL`. A user benched or

### 2026-08-25 — fix-test-cov-heap-flag (surfaced while shipping PR #1050)

- **[high — pre-existing CI config gap, NOT caused by that branch]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:36-46` — the `code` path filter lists `'package.json'` / `'package-lock.json'`, which in `dorny/paths-filter` match **only the repo-root files**. There is no `api/package.json` / `web/package.json` pattern in ANY filter (`code`, `api`, `web`, `contract` all miss them). Net effect: a change to a **workspace** `package.json` matches nothing, so `lint`, `unit-tests-api`, `unit-tests-web`, `integration-test-shard`, `smoke-test-shard` and the rest all report `skipping`, `mergeStateStatus` goes `CLEAN`, and auto-merge lands the change with **zero real CI**. Observed directly on PR #1050 (diff = `api/package.json` + `TECH-DEBT-BACKLOG.md`): every substantive job skipped, and `lint` skipping is the proof — `lint` is gated on `code`, so `code` evaluated false for `api/package.json`. That PR was still safe because the full gate had been run on the fleet first, but nothing in CI enforced that. Note this also partly undercuts the CLAUDE.md "Local CI — lite gate" rule for dependency changes: that rule says GitHub treats dependency files as `code` (so lint runs) but not `api`/`web` — true for the ROOT manifests, but for a **workspace** manifest not even lint runs. A workspace-only dep bump would land completely unverified. Suggested: add `'*/package.json'`, `'*/package-lock.json'` (or explicit `api/package.json`, `web/package.json`) to the `code` filter, and add `'api/package.json'` to `api` / `'web/package.json'` to `web` so unit+integration engage for a workspace dep change.

### 2026-08-29 — rok-1439-milestone-nominator-name (surfaced during the ROK-1439 pre-push review pass)

- **[med — pre-existing local tooling, NOT this branch]** The `codex` CLI (`/Users/sdodge/.nvm/versions/node/v24.11.1/bin/codex`) floods `codex review` stdout with a repeating startup error, once per exec step: `ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to refresh available models: stream disconnected before completion: failed to decode models response: unknown variant `max`, expected one of `none`, `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh``. Each occurrence dumps the **entire** server model-list JSON inline, so a review of a 2-file diff produced **3.4 MB / 327 KB of output across two runs** and the actual one-paragraph verdict was the very last line, unfindable without `grep -v`. The review itself completes and exits 0 — this is noise, not failure, which is what makes it expensive: the first run reads as a crash. Root cause is a client/server version skew — the deployed model list advertises a `max` reasoning effort that this CLI build's enum doesn't know. Evidence it is NOT this branch: the error fires during CLI startup before any diff is read, and is independent of repo state. Suggested: `npm i -g @openai/codex@latest` to pick up a build that knows the `max` variant; if the skew persists, have `.claude/skills/security-review` pipe through `grep -v 'codex_models_manager'` and print only the trailing verdict.
- ~~**[low — pre-existing, NOT this branch]** `codex review` self-selects verification commands and ran `cd api && npm run build -- --noEmit`, which fails immediately: `nest build` has no `--noEmit` option (`error: unknown option '--noEmit'`). Harmless — Codex recovered and re-ran plain `npm run build`, which succeeded — but it burns a step every review and the failed-command output reads like a branch breakage in the log. Separately, Codex's own `tsc` invocation reports hundreds of `Cannot find name 'it'` / `Cannot find name 'expect'` errors across `*.spec.ts` (e.g. `src/version/version.controller.spec.ts:44`) because it typechecks specs without the Jest types; the repo's own `npx tsc --noEmit -p api/tsconfig.json` is clean. Suggested: no code change — note in the security-review skill that Codex's ad-hoc build/tsc output is not the repo gate and should be ignored in favour of `validate-ci.sh`.~~ **FIXED 2026-10-02** — AGENTS.md "Typecheck commands (Codex reviews run read-only)" gives no-write repo-root typecheck forms (`npx tsc --noEmit --incremental false -p api/tsconfig.json`; the web app/node/test leaf configs), and the `security-review` Reporting rule now says a build/tsc/test failure Codex hit while self-verifying is not a branch finding.
### 2026-08-29 — rok-1440-public-lineup-invitees (surfaced during the ROK-1440 pre-push gate)

- ~~**[high — pre-existing gate bug, NOT this branch]** `scripts/validate-ci.sh:250-252` — `run_step()` maps **exit code 2 to `SKIPPED`**, so any step whose command exits 2 is silently reclassified as "not run" instead of failing the gate. `npm run build -w web` runs `tsc -b && vite build`, and **`tsc -b` exits 2 on a type error**, which npm surfaces as `npm error code 2`. Net effect: a genuinely broken web build prints its full error mid-run, is recorded as `Build (all workspaces): SKIPPED`, and the script still terminates with a green **`All checks passed!`** summary. Observed directly on this branch: `use-scheduling.ts` imported `addPollMembers` from the `api-client` barrel before the re-export existed, `npm run build -w web` failed with `error TS2305`, and `validate-ci.sh --static` reported all-clear. The failure was only caught because the SKIPPED row looked wrong for a diff that touches the contract and was investigated by hand — a less suspicious run would have pushed a branch that cannot build. Note the same reclassification applies to every step, so any tool that uses exit 2 for "real failure" is affected; `tsc -b` is simply the one in the default path. Evidence it is pre-existing: `git diff origin/main -- scripts/` is empty on this branch. Suggested: reserve a dedicated sentinel for skips (e.g. exit 77, or have skip-capable steps `echo` a marker) and treat 2 as a failure like any other non-zero code; at minimum, special-case the build/typecheck steps so `tsc`'s exit 2 cannot be read as a skip.~~ **FIXED 2026-08-30** — `run_step` no longer infers a skip from an exit code. A step now declares one explicitly via `skip_step` AND must exit cleanly, so a tool returning 2 for a real failure (`tsc`, `tsc -b`) can no longer be reported as SKIPPED. Regression test: `scripts/test/validate-ci-skip-contract.test.sh` (verified non-vacuous — 5 cases fail against the pre-fix script).
- ~~**[low — pre-existing, NOT this branch]** `npx tsc --noEmit -p web/tsconfig.json` does **not** reproduce the web build's type errors — it passed clean while `npm run build -w web` (`tsc -b`) failed with `TS2305` on a missing barrel export. The project-references build (`tsc -b`) is the authoritative check for the web workspace; a bare `--noEmit -p` run gives false confidence. Suggested: no code change, but agents should verify web type-safety with `npm run build -w web` (or `tsc -b`) rather than `tsc --noEmit -p web/tsconfig.json`; worth a line in `TESTING.md` or `CLAUDE.md` since the wrong invocation looks authoritative.~~ **FIXED 2026-10-01** — TESTING.md "Running Tests" now names `npm run build -w web` (`tsc -b`) as the web typecheck and warns off `-p web/tsconfig.json`.
- **[low — non-reproducible local integration flake, likely NOT this branch]** `api/src/lineups/lineups-matches.integration.spec.ts` failed **1 test of 19** on the first run of an 11-spec local batch (`Test Suites: 1 failed, 10 passed` / `Tests: 1 failed, 146 passed`), then passed **19/19 in isolation** and **147/147 on an identical re-run of the same batch**. Honest caveat: the failing assertion's message was **not captured** — the run was grepped for summary lines only, and the failure did not recur to be re-read, so this entry cannot name the failing case. Recorded rather than dismissed per the STRICT no-dismissal rule, and flagged as *likely* (not confirmed) another datapoint in the documented cross-suite carrier family (`reference_bullmq_ioredis_test_carrier`, `reference_integration_404_socket_carrier`, and the 2026-08-24 entries above), which has the same signature: a spec that is green alone and green on re-run failing once mid-batch. Branch context: this branch touches `lineups.controller.ts` invitee guards and adds `source: 'added'` to the match-member enum, neither of which `lineups-matches.integration.spec.ts` exercises, and GitHub CI shards integration 3×3 so it never accumulates the handles that produce this. Suggested: no action on its own; if it recurs, capture the full failure output (`--verbose`, no grep) before triaging, and add it to the carrier item rather than opening a new one.
- **[med — pre-existing CI smoke failure, NOT this branch]** `scripts/smoke/calendar.smoke.spec.ts:159-195` — `Regression: ROK-1315 — calendar shows gameless events when filter chip active › gameless event remains visible after the user opens the chip and clicks "None"` fails on GitHub `smoke-test-shard (1/5)` with `expect(locator).toBeVisible() failed` / `Error: element(s) not found` on `locator('.week-event-block').filter({ hasText: 'smoke-w1|w2|w3-gameless-event-remains-visible-<ts>-1-rok-1315-variety-night-2' }).first()` (15s timeout). Fails all three attempts; each retry seeds a fresh event (`w1` → `w2` → `w3`), so it is not a stale-fixture collision — the seeded gameless event simply never renders as a week block in time. Evidence it is pre-existing: the **identical** test is the sole failure of `smoke-test-shard (1/5)` on the latest `origin/main` CI run (run `33279909851`, head `82d313fc`, i.e. ROK-1439/PR #1058 post-merge), a commit this branch already contains; PR #1059 touches no calendar, events, or week-view code. Distinct from the `navigation.smoke.spec.ts` calendar race already tracked in `reference_known_smoke_flakes` — this one does not clear on rerun. Suggested: check whether the seeded event's start lands outside the rendered week window (the retry timestamps advance, so a boundary/TZ edge is plausible) before assuming a render race; do not add a blanket retry.

### 2026-08-30 — rok-validate-ci-skip-sentinel (surfaced while fixing the run_step skip bug)

- ~~**[med — pre-existing, NOT this branch]** `scripts/test/validate-ci-integration-shards.test.sh` fails 2 of 21 cases: `AC-M10-2` asserts each integration shard runs with `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=3072`, but `validate-ci.sh` actually uses **4096** on all four shards. Both the structural grep and the behavioral stub assertion fail. Verified pre-existing by stashing this branch's `validate-ci.sh` change and re-running: it fails identically against the unmodified script, and the assertion concerns heap sizing, not the skip mechanism this branch touches. Either the test or the script is stale — someone changed the heap ceiling (plausibly the same 8192 bump recorded in the 2026-08-25 entry above) without updating the AC. Suggested: decide which value is intended and align the other; until then `scripts/test/run-all.sh` cannot be green.~~ **FIXED 2026-08-30** — the stale AC was aligned to 4096 (the script is correct; ROK-1268 deliberately raised 3072→4096 and the assertion was never updated), the Docker-probe test now gates on daemon availability (skips locally, HARD-FAILS under CI so coverage can't be silently lost), `scripts/**` was added to the `code` path filter, and `./scripts/test/run-all.sh` now runs in the lint job. Suite is 10/10 in ~5s.
- ~~**[info]** `scripts/test/run-all.sh` is **not wired into CI** — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only runs `bash -n` over `scripts/*.sh`, so every behavioral shell test under `scripts/test/` is manual-only. That is why the `run_step` skip bug survived: a regression test there would not have caught it in CI regardless. Wiring the runner in is blocked on the failure above (it would make CI red on day one), so this branch adds its regression test alongside the existing ones and leaves the wiring decision to the operator.
