### 2026-09-05 — rok-1374-lifecycle (surfaced on PR #1099's CI discord-smoke, run 33992706567; report-only, not auto-filed)

- ~~**med (pre-existing flake class; not in the documented set)**~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-18 by ROK-1622 — it was never a flake class; it was a real bug, and the mechanism below diagnosed it correctly. Fixed on `fix/rok-1622-imminent-embed-state`: the initial post now derives its own state (`api/src/discord-bot/services/embed-state.helpers.ts`) and the embed-sync processor no longer treats a missing tracking row as success. `channel-embeds.test.ts:337` is correct as written and must NOT be weakened.** ~~`tools/test-bot/src/smoke/tests/channel-embeds.test.ts:337`~~ "Event embed chrome: per-state colour + author line (ROK-1460)" — `Expected embed color #f59e0b, got #38bdf8` (amber `needs_you` expected for a 60-minute-out event; cyan `announcing` received). The identical tip passed this test 32/32 on the previous CI run an hour earlier and on three fleet gates today. Mechanism: the initial post is always `EMBED_STATES.POSTED` (`api/src/discord-bot/services/embed-poster.service.ts:198` → `announcing`); the flip to `IMMINENT` (`needs_you`) happens only when the embed-sync processor recomputes state (`processors/embed-sync.helpers.ts:293`, `IMMINENT_THRESHOLD_MS` = 2 h) on a later sync, and nothing at creation time guarantees a sync lands before the smoke's first read — `awaitProcessing` drains the queues that exist at that moment, so whether the read sees amber depends on whether a sync was enqueued/ran in between. Not caused by this branch (no event-embed or embed-sync file in the diff).
  `Suggested:` either compute the state at post time (`embed-poster.service.ts` calling `computeEmbedState` so a 60-min-out event posts as IMMINENT directly) — the honest fix, and it removes an "announcing → needs_you" flicker users can see — or make the smoke poll `waitForEmbedUpdate` for the amber card instead of asserting the first read. Until then, treat this test as a rerun-once flake candidate.

### 2026-09-05 — rok-1478 (surfaced in review)

- **low** `web/src/pages/lfg/lfg-copy.ts:76` (`lookingLine`) — the group-page copy has drifted from the badge/banner copy that ROK-1478 unified. `groupLine` (`web/src/components/lfg/lfg-chip-copy.ts`) renders `N looking · needs M more` with `M = max(1, (viabilityThreshold ?? 2) - activeCount)`; `lookingLine` renders `N looking · needs M more` with an UNCLAMPED `viabilityThreshold - activeCount` and no `?? 2` fallback, so with no threshold configured it falls through to `1 looking — one more makes it a group` / `N looking` — a third sentence for the same fact. The same group can therefore read one way on its card badge and events banner and another way on its own page. ROK-1478 deliberately did not touch it (AC7 scoped the story out of `web/src/pages/lfg/**`, and adopting `groupLine` would have changed the group page's rendered copy without an approved target). `Suggested:` pick ONE sentence with the operator, then have `lookingLine` delegate to `groupLine` (or delete it) so the three surfaces cannot disagree again.
- **nit** `web/src/components/lfg/lfg-chip.tsx:73` — redundant `role="link"` on an element that is already an `<a href>`. It was load-bearing while the chip was a `<button role="link">` (ROK-1453 D5); ROK-1478 AC4 made it a real anchor, and the attribute now only survives because `lfg-chip.test.tsx:160-169` pins it directly. Harmless (the implicit and explicit roles agree, and axe is clean), but it is a redundant ARIA role of the kind the house lint would otherwise flag. `Suggested:` drop the attribute and retarget that assertion at `getByRole('link', { name })`, which is the property the test actually cares about.

### 2026-09-05 — rok-1478 (surfaced by the walk-fix fleet gate `9581fb63c347`; report-only, not auto-filed)

- **med (pre-existing on `origin/main`; env-data dependent)** `api/src/igdb/igdb-search.helpers.ts:84` (+ `igdb-search-executor.helpers.ts:91,:123`) — the local games search applies `IGDB_CONFIG.SEARCH_LIMIT` (20) in the DB query BEFORE `sortByRelevance`, so once a name family has more than 20 rows the canonical row can fall outside the limited set and never be sorted to the top. On fleet env `rok-1478` the smoke fixtures have accumulated **22** `World of Warcraft*` rows (DB query 2026-09-05), and `game-badges-personalization.smoke.spec.ts:313` / `:327` fail deterministically in both projects: `expect(locator).toBeVisible() failed / Locator: locator('a[href="/games/1"]:visible').first() / Timeout: 20000ms / element(s) not found` at `openLibraryFor` (`:154`) — game 1 IS "World of Warcraft" but is not in the first 20. The same signature hit the ROK-1471 env gate the same day (4 failures) and flaked once on the earlier ROK-1478 gate; the branch diff has zero `api/src/igdb` changes and the walk fixes touch no library code. Not a story regression.
  `Suggested:` rank before limiting — apply `sortByRelevance` (exact/prefix match first) inside the query (`ORDER BY (name ILIKE q) DESC, similarity DESC LIMIT 20`) or fetch `limit * 3` candidates and slice after sorting; separately, the smoke should search a worker-unique fixture name rather than the most-cloned family in the catalogue.

### 2026-09-06 — fix/rok-1393 (surfaced during fix-batch)

- **med (pre-existing; infra file, out of scope for the fix)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:971-972` — the `docker-build` job that publishes the daily `main` image Watchtower pulls passes only `COMMIT_SHA=${{ github.sha }}` as a build-arg; `APP_VERSION` is passed only by `.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml:49-50`, which fires solely on `v*` tag pushes (`:3-6`). `Dockerfile.allinone:564-565` therefore bakes `APP_VERSION=''`, `VersionCheckService` (`api/src/version/version-check.service.ts`) falls through to `/app/package.json`, and `GET /system/version` reports `0.1.1` in prod. ROK-1393 stops this from producing the false "out of date" banner (commit-vs-main comparison), but the public `/system/version` string stays wrong on every daily build. Not caused by this branch. `Suggested:` pass `APP_VERSION=${{ github.ref_name }}`-equivalent (e.g. the short sha or `main-<sha>`) in ci.yml's docker-build `build-args`, or have `getVersion()` return the short COMMIT_SHA when APP_VERSION is empty — infra PR, own story.
- **low (pre-existing)** `api/package.json:3` — `"version": "0.1.1"` has never been bumped (`git log -S'"version": "' -- api/package.json` shows only the initial commit) while the newest release tag is `v1.1.0` and the root `package.json` says `1.1.0`. `Dockerfile.allinone:163` copies `api/package.json` to `/app/package.json`, so this is the value every semver fallback reads. `release.yml` bumps it on tagged releases, but the existing tags predate/bypass it. `Suggested:` one-line bump to match the root version, or make the fallback read the root package.json in the image.

### 2026-09-06 — fix/batch-2026-09-06 (surfaced during the fix-batch fleet gate; report-only, not auto-filed)

- **[med — Playwright harness defect on the fleet path, NOT this branch]** `scripts/smoke/community-lineup.smoke.spec.ts:446` and `scripts/smoke/events.smoke.spec.ts:282` (mobile project only) failed on fleet gate `0c12ebb0ab6c` with `Error: Error reading storage state from scripts/.auth/admin.json: ENOENT: no such file or directory, open 'scripts/.auth/admin.json'` — not these specs' documented desktop signatures. The storage-state path is resolved relative to the process cwd rather than `playwright.config.ts`, so a mobile worker started from another cwd (or after global-setup's auth file was written elsewhere) cannot find the admin session. Branch touches no smoke/auth harness. Suggested: resolve `storageState` with `path.resolve(__dirname, ...)` (or `fileURLToPath`) in the config/projects, and have global-setup write the same absolute path; then re-check whether the two documented desktop reds for these files share the root cause.
- **[low — companion-smoke flake, NOT this branch]** `tools/test-bot/src/smoke/tests/*` `ROK-1351: text + voice series bindings both persist (AC4)` — `Text slot expected channelType=text, got voice` after 3.7 s on fleet task `a675fc38f388` (slot 2, scoped `--only-e2e`), and the identical assertion on GitHub `Discord Smoke Tests` run `34066383983` for `rok-1479` the same night (a branch without this batch's changes). The existing L308 entry files it under the local-host timeout family (100–400 s), so this fast-assertion shape was not covered; `handover-ROK-1474.md:733` has the same test passing on the fleet at 3.3 s. Off the ROK-1456 surface (series bindings, not ad-hoc suppression). Suggested: read the AC4 fixture — the text/voice slot pair is probably read back in insertion order rather than by `channelType`, so a reordered read flips the assertion; assert by type, not position.
- **[med — fleet tooling gap, NOT this branch]** `rl-infra/**` image builds (`rl_env_build_image_from_runner` / `rl_env_deploy`) pass no `COMMIT_SHA` (or `APP_VERSION`) build-arg, so every fleet env reports `GET /system/version → {"commitSha": null}` (verified on `https://slot-2.gamernight.net`, 2026-09-07). Consequence: ROK-1393's main-vs-running comparison can never be exercised on a fleet env — the check takes the semver fallback (`0.1.1` vs the latest release tag) and the admin banner shows "out of date" there exactly as it did in prod before the fix. The only surface that runs the real path is the CI-built image (ci.yml passes `COMMIT_SHA=${{ github.sha }}`). Suggested: have the runner image build pass `--build-arg COMMIT_SHA=$(git -C /workspace rev-parse HEAD)` (and `APP_VERSION` from the synced worktree's nearest tag) so fleet envs are honest about what they run; then the banner is walkable on the fleet.

### 2026-09-05 — rok-1483-web (surfaced while wiring the ROK-1483 web lane; report-only, not auto-filed)

- ~~**high (pre-existing on `origin/main`)** `scripts/validate-ci.sh:628` — the web TypeScript gate typechecks **nothing**. `npx tsc --noEmit -p web/tsconfig.json` points at a SOLUTION-style config (`{"files": [], "references": [tsconfig.app.json, tsconfig.node.json]}`); with `-p` (not `-b`) tsc compiles the project's own file list, which is empty, and does not follow references. Verified by dropping `web/src/__tsc_probe.ts` containing `export const _probe: number = "not a number";` — `-p web/tsconfig.json` exits **0 with no output**, while `-p web/tsconfig.app.json` reports `error TS2322: Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'number'`. Web type errors are today caught only incidentally by the separate `npm run build` step (`tsc -b`, which does follow references), so any `validate-ci.sh` invocation that builds nothing — or any agent quoting the tsc step as its typecheck evidence, which several briefs do verbatim — is reading a green light from a no-op. `git diff origin/main -- scripts/validate-ci.sh web/tsconfig*.json` is empty, so this is not from this branch. `Suggested:` change line 628 to `npx tsc --noEmit -p web/tsconfig.app.json` (add a second line for `tsconfig.node.json`), or switch it to `npx tsc -b web/tsconfig.json --force`.~~ **FIXED 2026-09-26** — `run_typecheck` now runs the leaf configs `web/tsconfig.app.json`, `web/tsconfig.node.json` and `web/tsconfig.test.json` (validate-ci.sh:660-663), #1375.
- ~~**med (pre-existing on `origin/main`; consequence of the above)** `web/tsconfig.app.json:35` — `"exclude": ["src/**/*.test.ts", "src/**/*.test.tsx", "src/test/**"]` means **no gate anywhere typechecks a web test file**: `tsc -b` skips them by exclusion and vitest transpiles without checking. This is the web twin of the documented `ts-jest does not typecheck` trap, and it silently defeats type-level test assertions — ROK-1483 AC6 specifies an `Expect<Equal<keyof Props, …>>` pin *in the spec file*, which would have been decorative there (the pin was moved into `threaded-chat-viewer.tsx` and proven to fail with `TS2344: Type 'false' does not satisfy the constraint 'true'`). It also means a spec fixture that drifts from a shared DTO compiles forever. `Suggested:` add a `web/tsconfig.test.json` (extends app, includes the test globs, `types: ["vitest/globals"]`) and typecheck it in the same gate step; expect a one-off cleanup of existing drift.~~ **FIXED 2026-10-02** — web specs are typechecked by `web/tsconfig.test.json`, gated in `validate-ci.sh` `run_typecheck` (:663) and the CI lint job's "Typecheck (web specs)" step (ci.yml:199-200), #1427.

### 2026-09-06 — rok-1474 (surfaced during the ROK-1474 API review fix lane)

- **[med]** `api/src/lineups/lineups-voting.helpers.ts:66-86` (`toggleVote`) — the approval-vote write is an unserialised read-then-write inside a transaction: `findExistingVote` → (delete | `countUserVotes` cap check → `insertVote`), with no advisory lock and no `ON CONFLICT`. Under READ COMMITTED, transaction B cannot see A's uncommitted insert, so two concurrent votes by the same voter (a double-click, or a client retry) both miss `findExistingVote` and both insert. Two shapes result: same game → `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "uq_lineup_vote_user_game"` surfacing as an unhandled HTTP 500 rather than a sane toggle result; different games → both cap checks read `count = maxVotes - 1` and the voter ends up one vote OVER `maxVotesPerPlayer`, with no constraint to catch it. This is exactly the shape the star write had before `531c2888`, which fixed its twin by taking `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on `(lineupId, userId)` as the transaction's first statement (`lockVoterStar`, same file). **Pre-existing, not introduced by ROK-1474:** `git diff origin/main..HEAD -- api/src/lineups/lineups-voting.helpers.ts` shows no change inside `toggleVote`'s body — the branch only adds `setStar` and its lock alongside it; the reviewer independently noted the twin at review-ROK-1474-api.md section 3 ("The same shape exists in the untouched `toggleVote`"). Deliberately NOT fixed here: it predates the story, and the vote path has its own integration coverage that a serialisation change should be re-run against.
  Suggested: call the existing `lockVoterStar(tx, lineupId, userId)` (rename it `lockVoterBallot`) as `toggleVote`'s first statement so both writes on the same voter's ballot share one advisory-lock key. Do NOT wrap the insert in try/catch — under postgres.js a failed statement poisons the whole transaction, savepoints included (memory `reference_postgres_savepoint_does_not_contain_violations.md`).

### 2026-09-06 — rok-1474 (surfaced during the ROK-1474 fleet ship gate; report-only, not auto-filed)

- **med (fleet shard flake; pre-existing — file untouched by the branch)** `api/src/backup/backup.integration.spec.ts:459` — `restored dump has 0 rows in the 4 sanitized tables, > 0 in games` failed ONCE on the fleet (gate task `0065d7789ded`, integration shard 1/4 of 41 suites) with `expect(received).toBe(expected) · Expected: 0 · Received: 4` on the first table the loop checks (`app_settings`). Not reproducible in isolation: alone (task `4ec61709c313`), and twice as `lineup-tie-hold → backup` in one `--runInBand` invocation with a forced sequencer (`25a8845536c1`, `e7545b6f3309`) — 27/27 green each time. Code reads rule out the obvious writers: `truncateAllTables` deletes every public table, restore runs `pg_restore --clean`, `bootstrapAdmin` writes users/credentials only, `emitBotReconnect` needs a token already in the cache. What remains is a race between the count and something writing `app_settings` during the 41-suite shard (a leaked handler from an earlier suite — the ROK-1268 ioredis/BullMQ carrier family — or a cron writer), which only the full shard reproduces. GitHub CI has never shown it. `Suggested:` make the assertion race-proof — snapshot the four counts immediately after `pg_restore` inside the restore path under test, or have the test count rows whose `created_at` predates the restore; and add `--detectOpenHandles` to the shard runner so a leaked writer is named instead of inferred.
- **med (fleet-path Playwright; pre-existing — main CI green at `3ab490ab`)** `scripts/smoke/lineup-tie-readiness.smoke.spec.ts:168` — `the game-detail banner names the tie instead of the plain vote banner (AC13)`: `getByText(/Tied — waiting on .+ to pick/)` not found, desktop AND mobile, deterministic on the fleet (ROK-1474 gate `102662cfaf13` incl. retries; ROK-1479 gate `d967a1d92269` + rerun `ffb9bd50afe2` — a branch that touches no lineup code), while the same tip's GitHub CI Playwright passed. The banner is community-wide (`/lineups/banner`; already the reason this spec sits in the rerun-once set) and the fleet runs every spec in one worker pool, so a sibling spec's lineup takes the slot. `Suggested:` scope the banner assertion to the test's own lineup (assert on the lineup id in the banner link rather than on the first banner rendered), or serialise the lineup-banner specs on the fleet. Promoted from rerun-once to the fleet-path set for classification.
- **med (companion smoke test race + matcher; pre-existing — file untouched by the branch)** `tools/test-bot/src/smoke/tests/private-lineup.test.ts:347-367` (`Private lineup nomination milestone … (ROK-1115)`) failed twice on the fleet (`cc4731e0d541`, `9a8a3d4866cf`) with its own message while passing on the ROK-1479 env. Embed dump (handover `### ROK-1115 embed dump`): the matched embed was the **ABORTED** card for the test's own lineup (author `🛑 ABORTED`, footer `Raid Ledger · Aborted`, posted 03:01:53.58Z, 5.9 s after create — INSIDE the 8 s `assertConditionNeverMet` window), aborted by a sibling test's global `archiveAllLineups()` under `SMOKE_CONCURRENCY=5` (six ABORTED cards for six private lineups in one 11 s burst). Two defects: (a) the matcher `/milestone|nominations filled|nominated/i` runs over title+author+description and the fixture's own title contains "Milestone", so it can never discriminate — match on the author line / footer family instead; (b) `archiveAllLineups()` is global and races every concurrent lineup test. `Suggested:` structural matcher (`author` === the milestone family) + scope the archive helper to lineups the calling test created (title prefix or a per-run tag).
- **product question (operator)** — the ABORTED card for a `visibility='private'` lineup is posted to the PUBLIC default channel (`lineup-notification.service.ts:322` "Post the channel-level Aborted embed. No DMs", ROK-1062). Either the ABORTED family intentionally skips private suppression or it is a leak of a private lineup's title into the channel. Observed for lineups 11, 8, 7, 6 on 2026-09-06; pre-existing, not ROK-1474.

### 2026-09-06 — rok-1474 (surfaced on the GitHub CI rerun for PR #1103; report-only, not auto-filed)

- **[med — CI discord-smoke flake, NOT this branch]** `tools/test-bot/src/smoke/tests/voice-activity.test.ts:792` — `Voice classification populates attendance and metrics (ROK-943)` failed on GitHub run `34066169266` (the `--failed` rerun of PR #1103) with `attended: expected 4, got 0` after 2.8 s; the three other voice-suite tests passed and the same test passed on the three most recent `origin/main` runs (`33999235134`, `33998094908`, `33989837249`). The branch touches no voice/attendance/discord-bot source (its only smoke change is `lineup-tie.test.ts`). Mechanism suspected from the test shape: `rok943AssertMetrics` reads `/events/:id/metrics` exactly once after `triggerClassify` + `awaitProcessing`; an `attended` of 0 with `total` populated means the classification write had not landed when the single read ran (the seven signups and five injected sessions were all accepted, or the earlier steps would have thrown first). Suggested: poll the metrics read with `pollForCondition` until `attendanceSummary.attended > 0` (bounded by the suite timeout) instead of one read after `await-processing`, and log the queue drain result; do not add a retry to the classify call.

Pre-existing on `origin/main`; not touched by this branch. `Suggested:` filter with `ch.type === ChannelType.GuildText` (or `ChannelType.GuildText | GuildAnnouncement`) in `listGuildTextChannels`, add `type` to the DTO, and give the settings page a one-line unit pin that a GuildVoice channel is absent from the text list.

### 2026-09-07 — rok-1457 (surfaced while unblocking PR #1111)

- ~~**[RESOLVED 2026-09-08 — WAS med]** `.github/workflows/discord-smoke.yml:18-20` — the `discord-smoke-shared-guild` concurrency group is declared at **workflow level**, so it is claimed by *every* run of this workflow, including runs where `detect-changes` reports `discord == 'false'` and the `discord-smoke` job is skipped outright. GitHub keeps at most **one pending run per group**, and a newly queued run evicts (cancels) the pending one — so a dependabot PR that touches only root `package.json` can queue behind a running smoke and **cancel a real PR's pending run**. A cancelled run leaves no conclusion, so the required `discord-smoke` context simply disappears and the PR sits at `BLOCKED` with nothing red to explain it. Observed today: PR #1111 (ROK-1455) was fully green at 12:21Z, had its smoke run cancelled at 12:55Z by dependabot pushes (#1118/#1119/#1120), and stayed unmergeable until a manual `gh run rerun` at 16:56Z — the branch was never at fault. `Suggested:` move the `concurrency:` block off the workflow and onto the `discord-smoke` job, so only runs that actually drive the shared guild contend for it; runs whose smoke job is skipped then never enter — let alone evict — the queue. `cancel-in-progress: false` stays as-is. **[RESOLVED 2026-09-08 by `745c1a5e` / PR #1121 — the text above describes behaviour that no longer exists; do not re-derive it.]** The `concurrency:` block was moved off the workflow and onto the `discord-smoke` job exactly as suggested; it now lives at `.github/workflows/discord-smoke.yml:64-66` (indented under the job, with the rationale comment at lines 45-63) and there is no top-level `concurrency:` key in the file. Only runs whose `discord-smoke` job actually executes contend for the shared guild. Known residual, documented in the commit's own comment and NOT a regression: three genuinely Discord-touching PRs at once can still evict one another's pending run — narrowing the population does not deepen GitHub's one-slot queue.~~ — **struck 2026-09-23 (ROK-1620): concurrency is job-level since PR #1121.**

### 2026-09-07 — rok-1494 (surfaced while merging origin/main into the story branch)

- **low** `api/src/drizzle/migrations/meta/0102_snapshot.json:3` and `api/src/drizzle/migrations/meta/0112_snapshot.json:3` — the Drizzle snapshot `prevId` chain is broken at two historical points. `0102_lineup_phase_scheduling` has `"prevId": "0101_regular_adam_destine"` (a migration **tag string** where a UUID belongs), and `0112_flat_sharon_ventura` has `"prevId": "e860e56c-7059-41d9-b866-4b00fdf09b81"` where the preceding snapshot's `id` is `c2ae4ed3-9f5b-4518-8139-da62f70d9a2c`. Confirmed **pre-existing on `origin/main`** by walking the journal against `git show origin/main:<snapshot>` — both breaks reproduce on a clean main checkout, neither is touched by this branch. Harmless at runtime: the programmatic migrator (`api/src/scripts/run-migrations.ts`) executes SQL in `_journal.json` order and never reads `prevId`; only `drizzle-kit generate` walks the chain, so the risk is a future `generate` producing a bad diff from the wrong ancestor. `Suggested:` one-off repair setting each `prevId` to the preceding snapshot's `id`, plus a `scripts/` chain-walk assertion alongside `fix-migration-order.sh --check` so the next break fails CI instead of being rediscovered.

### 2026-09-09 — rok-1494 (surfaced while triaging PR #1112's red CI; report-only, not auto-filed)

- **med (GitHub CI infrastructure; pre-existing — unrelated to any branch under test)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the `Install Playwright browsers` step in `smoke-test-shard`. On PR #1112 run `34368386095`, shard `(2/5)` failed with `##[error]The action 'Install Playwright browsers' has timed out after 5 minutes.` **No spec executed** — the shard died during browser download, and the step's own `for i in 1 2 3` retry wrapper exhausted all three attempts inside the same 5-minute action timeout, so the retry is decorative: one slow chromium fetch consumes the whole budget and the remaining attempts never get room to run. Surfaced on `rok-1457`, whose diff is `api/src/lfg/**` + `web/src/**` and touches no Playwright config, so it is not branch-caused; the identical step passes on the other four shards of the same run. `Suggested:` give the step its own `timeout-minutes` larger than the retry budget (or drop the inner retry and use the step-level retry), and cache the browser download so a cold CDN fetch is not on the critical path of every shard.

- **med (GitHub CI infrastructure; pre-existing — second failure of this class today)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the `integration-test-shard` step `sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y postgresql-client` (needed for `pg_dump` in `backup.integration.spec.ts`). On PR #1123 run `34383498439` **all four shards** failed identically at 17:39Z, before a single test ran: `E: Failed to fetch https://dl.google.com/linux/chrome-stable/deb/dists/stable/main/binary-amd64/Packages.gz  Hash Sum mismatch` → `E: Some index files failed to download` → `##[error]Process completed with exit code 100`. `apt-get update` refreshes EVERY configured source, and the GitHub runner image ships a Google Chrome list; a transient hash mismatch in that unrelated third-party repo therefore hard-fails a step that only wants `postgresql-client` from the distro repo. Nothing in the integration suite uses Chrome. Not branch-caused — the diff is `rl-infra/**` + `api/src/discord-bot/**` and touches no workflow or dependency file; all four shards fail together, which is the signature of a shared setup step, not of test code. `Suggested:` drop the third-party lists before updating (`sudo rm -f /etc/apt/sources.list.d/google-chrome.list`) or scope the refresh to the distro sources (`-o Dir::Etc::sourcelist=/etc/apt/sources.list -o Dir::Etc::sourceparts=-`), and add `-o Acquire::Retries=3`. Same family as the `Install Playwright browsers` 5-minute timeout documented above: **both are third-party CDN fetches on the critical path of a job that could cache or avoid them.** **Also hit `origin/main` itself the same afternoon:** run `34381433560` on `5ae7aa39` (bullmq 6.3.4, PR #1127) failed **19 jobs** — every integration shard, every smoke shard and both aggregates — all in the same setup step, across three retry attempts (`Failed to install browsers / Installation process exited with code: 100`). **bullmq is exonerated by evidence, not assumption:** PR #1122's run on `b1fadcf3` already contained `5ae7aa39`/bullmq 6.3.4 and passed 4/4 integration and 5/5 smoke shards in the same window. Do NOT open a bullmq regression story off run `34381433560`. A repo-wide red run from this cause is indistinguishable at a glance from a real regression, which is the second reason to fix it. `mirror-ci-images.yml` already exists — installing browsers from a pinned/mirrored image instead of apt-from-dl.google.com would remove the shared point of failure entirely.

### 2026-09-10 — fix-batch/activity-rollups (surfaced during ROK-1465)

- **med (same flake as the 2026-09-07 entry at `backup.integration.spec.ts:459`; NOT a duplicate — it records the first GitHub CI occurrence)** The `restored dump has 0 rows in the 4 sanitized tables, > 0 in games` race has now been observed on **GitHub CI**, which the earlier entry explicitly noted it never had ("GitHub CI has never shown it"). PR #1144, run `34435759234`, `integration-test-shard (2/4, 1)`, 43 suites: `expect(received).toBe(expected) · Expected: 0 · Received: 4` at line 459 — identical signature, and again on the first table the loop checks (`app_settings`). It failed on both jest attempts within that job (04:13:35Z and 04:18:36Z), so the in-job retry does not clear it. **Not branch-caused:** ROK-1465's diff is `game_activity_rollups` aggregation only and writes to none of the four sanitized tables (`app_settings`, `local_credentials`, `sessions`, `consumed_intent_tokens`); PR #1145 passed the same shard number in the same window; and locally the new spec and `backup.integration.spec.ts` pass together in **both** orders (29/29 each way). What the branch *does* change is shard composition — adding `game-activity-rollup.integration.spec.ts` shifts which suites land in shard 2/4, and CI runs integration with `--randomize`, so a different neighbour set plus a different file order is exactly the perturbation the earlier entry predicted would be needed to hit the race. `Suggested:` unchanged from the 2026-09-07 entry — make the assertion race-proof (snapshot the four counts inside the restore path, or count only rows whose `created_at` predates the restore) and run the shard with `--detectOpenHandles` so the leaked `app_settings` writer is named rather than inferred. Now that it reproduces on GitHub CI, this is worth doing before it starts red-flagging unrelated PRs at merge time.

### 2026-09-10 — fix-batch/rok-1138-followups (surfaced while generating a migration for ROK-1148)

- **med (Drizzle snapshot drift; pre-existing on `origin/main` — the recurrence the 2026-09-07 rok-1506 entry predicted)** `api/src/drizzle/migrations/meta/0177_snapshot.json` was generated from a base predating **0176**, so it contains **zero** references to `lfg_invites` (`grep -c lfg_invites` → 0176: 17, **0177: 0**, 0178: 17). Consequence: `npm run db:generate -w api` on top of 0177 re-emits the whole of 0176 into every new migration. Observed verbatim generating 0178 — `CREATE TABLE "lfg_invites"`, its three FK constraints, its three indexes, and the `user_notification_preferences.channel_prefs` default that 0176 already sets identically — with the one genuinely new statement (`CREATE INDEX idx_ai_request_logs_provider_success_created_at`) appended last. Applying that unedited would fail with `relation "lfg_invites" already exists` on any database that has run 0176. **Not branch-caused:** reproduced from a clean `origin/main` checkout; this branch touches only `ai-request-logs.ts`. Handled here the same way ROK-1506 handled the 0175 instance — hand-trimmed `0178_shallow_omega_sentinel.sql` to its single statement (documented in the file header and the commit) and kept the freshly generated `0178_snapshot.json`, which carries the full schema again, so branches cut from here no longer inherit the drift. `./scripts/validate-migrations.sh` then applied all 178 migrations cleanly against a real Postgres. **This is now the second confirmed instance in three days, and the earlier entry's "verify 0176 too" suggestion was not enough — the stale snapshot moved to 0177.** `Suggested:` stop relying on the next author noticing. Add a CI check that regenerates from HEAD and fails when `drizzle-kit generate` emits any statement not attributable to the current diff (the 2026-09-07 entry proposed the same thing as a `validate-migrations.sh` dry-run diff); short of that, a `--check` mode that asserts each snapshot contains every table created by earlier migrations would catch the drift at the source instead of one migration later.

### 2026-09-11 — claude/unblock-prs-86e246 (surfaced during /unblock-prs queue triage)

- **high (GitHub CI infrastructure; pre-existing on `origin/main` — the integration heap-OOM has crossed from local into CI shards)** `api/jest.integration.config.js` + `.github/workflows/ci.yml` `integration-test-shard`. On `origin/main@c3f228ef` (PR #1137, already merged) run `34531965900`, shards `(1/4, 1)` and `(4/4, 2)` died with `FATAL ERROR: Ineffective mark-compacts near heap limit Allocation failed - JavaScript heap out of memory` → `Aborted (core dumped)` → `##[error]Process completed with exit code 134`, after ~439s of GC thrash at `3766.9 (4122.4) MB` against the script's own `NODE_OPTIONS="--max-old-space-size=4096"`. **This invalidates the standing assumption recorded in `reference_bullmq_ioredis_test_carrier` and in the 2026-08-15 backlog entry — "GitHub CI shards so it never hits."** It now hits. Sharding reduced the per-process accumulation but did not bound it, and the CI budget (4GB) is *half* the local workaround already documented as insufficient (8GB). **Not branch-caused:** nothing was under test — this is `main` itself; the other 10 of 12 shard jobs in the same run passed, the two immediately preceding main commits (`155675ce`, `f7cd902e`) were green, and a `gh run rerun --failed` cleared it. The failure mode is memory exhaustion, not an assertion. **CORRECTION (same day, after the rerun completed): this is NOT a flake and the rerun did NOT clear it.** Shard `(4/4, 2)` passed on re-run, but shard `(1/4, 1)` **OOM'd again, identically** — job `103295061842`, same `Ineffective mark-compacts` → `Aborted (core dumped)` → exit 134, at the same ~434s, after the same **43 suites passed**, with heap pinned at `3764.8 (4127.4) MB` against the same 4096MB cap. Both runs died immediately after `src/settings/reencrypt-settings.integration.spec.ts`, which is the carrier, not the cause (the leak is monotonic across the shard; whichever suite is running when the cap is reached is the one blamed). **`origin/main` is therefore deterministically red on integration shard 1/4 and every PR branching from it inherits a red required check.** Treat this as a blocker, not as backlog. **RESOLVED 2026-09-11 by the Node 22 migration (ROK-1526, PR #1160) — see the resolution entry at the end of this file. `origin/main` CI is green again; do NOT treat this entry as a live blocker.** `Suggested:` (a) immediate — raise `--max-old-space-size` for the sharded CI invocation above 4096 so a merge queue is not gated on GC luck; (b) real fix, unchanged from the 2026-08-15 entry — run with `--detectOpenHandles` and close the leaking BullMQ/ioredis handles in `afterAll`, since the leak is what makes heap growth monotonic across suites regardless of shard width. Worth prioritising now that it red-flags `main` and not just local runs: an OOM on main is indistinguishable at a glance from a real regression, and the reflex fix ("rerun") trains agents to re-run red CI, which every other STRICT rule in this repo forbids.

- **med (blocked dependency, not a defect; caused by PR #1142's own diff — recorded so the next agent does not re-triage it)** `api/package.json` — dependabot PR #1142 (`@testcontainers/postgresql` 11.14.0 → 12.1.0) **cannot pass CI on this repo's Node version and should not be retried.** Run `34531984173`: all four integration shards fail at import time, before any test, with `TypeError: webidl.util.markAsUncloneable is not a function` at `node_modules/testcontainers/node_modules/undici/lib/web/cache/cachestorage.js:20:17`, reached from `common/testing/test-app.ts:27` → `@testcontainers/postgresql`. Root cause is an engine mismatch, not a flake: `testcontainers@12.1.0` declares `engines: {"node": ">= 22.22"}` and bundles `undici@8.10.2` (`engines: {"node": ">=22.19.0"}`), while every CI job pins `node-version: 20` (resolved 20.20.2) and both `Dockerfile.allinone` and `api/Dockerfile` build on `node:20-alpine`. No workspace declares an `engines` field, so npm installed a package the runtime cannot execute without erroring. `Suggested:` keep `@testcontainers/postgresql` pinned at `^11.x` (a dependabot `ignore` entry for major versions of that package would stop the PR reappearing) until the repo migrates to Node 22 — that migration is its own infrastructure story (two Dockerfiles + every workflow pin + an allinone container validation per the STRICT infra rules), not a devDependency bump. Adding an explicit `"engines": {"node": "20.x"}` to the root `package.json` would also make npm refuse this class of install up front rather than deferring the failure to a red CI run.

### 2026-09-11 — rok-1526 (resolution of the integration heap-OOM documented earlier today)

- **RESOLVED — the integration heap-OOM was a Node 20 problem, and Node 22 fixed it.** Earlier today this file recorded the OOM first as environmental, then corrected it to deterministic after shard `(1/4, 1)` reproduced it on rerun. Both entries stand as written; this one records the outcome. The operator directed a Node 20 → 22 migration (ROK-1526, PR #1160), and the same shard on the same 4096MB cap went from *dying* to *passing with no GC pressure at all*:

  | | Node 20 (`c3f228ef`, twice) | Node 22 (`#1160`) |
  | -- | -- | -- |
  | Suites | 43 passed, **OOM on the 44th** | **44 / 44 passed** |
  | Tests | died before reporting | **463 passed** |
  | Wall time | ~434s → `Aborted (core dumped)`, exit 134 | **236s**, clean exit |
  | `mark-compacts` / allocation-failure lines | many | **zero** |
  | `reencrypt-settings.integration.spec.ts` | the OOM victim, both runs | **PASS** |

  `origin/main` CI on `f099dac8` (the first main commit on Node 22) completed **success**. The previous main commit `6ecc41a4` was still red.

  **The carrier was never the cause, and the leak is NOT fixed.** `reencrypt-settings` took the blame both times only because the leak is monotonic across the shard and that suite happened to be running when the cap was reached. Node 22's allocator absorbs the same allocation pattern with large headroom — it did not close the leak. Jest still prints `Force exiting Jest: Have you considered using --detectOpenHandles` on Node 22 runs, i.e. the BullMQ/ioredis open-handle leak recorded in `reference_bullmq_ioredis_test_carrier` is **still live**. It is no longer fatal, which downgrades it from blocker to `low` — but a future suite-count increase could push it back over even an 8192 cap. The real fix (`--detectOpenHandles`, close handles in `afterAll`) remains the standing recommendation.

  **Note on the 8192 stopgap (PR #1161):** the integration heap cap was raised 4096 → 8192 the same afternoon, before #1160 landed. It is *not* what fixed this — Node 22 passed the shard with zero GC distress against the **old** 4096 cap. Harmless belt-and-braces on a 16GB runner; keep or revert at the operator's discretion, but do not read it later as the cause of the fix.

- **[note — not a defect]** `@testcontainers/postgresql` 12.x (PR #1142) no longer fails with `TypeError: webidl.util.markAsUncloneable is not a function` once Node 22 is in place: that signature is **absent** from the post-migration logs and 425/426 tests in shard `4/4` pass. The one remaining failure is `connect ECONNRESET 127.0.0.1:<port>` in `LFG board ⇄ web chips parity (ROK-1505 AC4)` — a transport error, not an assertion, and the same socket-carrier family as `reference_integration_404_socket_carrier` / ROK-1091. Rerun-class, not a testcontainers-12 regression. **^ THAT CALL WAS WRONG — corrected below the same day; do not act on it.**

### 2026-09-11 — rok-1526 follow-up (correcting the same-day call on PR #1142)

- **high (real regression in `@testcontainers/postgresql` 12.x — PR #1142 must NOT be merged as-is)** The entry above called shard `4/4`'s `connect ECONNRESET` on #1142 "rerun-class, not a testcontainers-12 regression." **That was wrong, and it was called before the rerun evidence existed.** The controlled comparison — same Node 22, same shard composition, the ONLY variable being the testcontainers major — says the opposite:

  | | `origin/main` (testcontainers **11**) | PR #1142 (testcontainers **12**) |
  | -- | -- | -- |
  | `src/discord-bot/lfg-board/lfg-board-parity.integration.spec.ts` | **PASS** (5.259 s) | **FAIL** (5.318 s) |
  | Shard 4/4 totals | 43/43 suites, **426/426** tests | 42/43 suites, **425/426** tests |
  | Runs observed | green (run `34620217019`, and all 4 shards green on #1160) | **failed twice** — runs `34620235607` + `34622378006` |
  | Jest `--randomize` seed | -918251065 | **-939271068 and 511995713** (different seeds, identical failure) |

  **Why "flake" is ruled out:** two different randomize seeds produced the same failure in the same spec. Order-dependent flake does not survive a reseed. The spec's own runtime is unchanged (5.26s → 5.32s), so it is not a timeout or a slow-runner effect, and the failure is a transport error rather than an assertion — the test never gets its answer, it loses the connection.

  **The lead worth chasing first:** the error is `connect ECONNRESET 127.0.0.1:<high port>` and the port differs per run (40597, then 46657) — that is the shape of an ephemeral **Testcontainers-mapped host port**, i.e. the suite's connection to its own Postgres container being reset, which is exactly the surface testcontainers 12 changed. No `ryuk`/`reaper` lines appear in the job log, so the reaper is not visibly announcing a kill; container lifecycle//teardown timing under the new major is the first thing to instrument. `testcontainers@12` also declares `engines: node >= 22.22` and bundles `undici@8`, so its HTTP/socket stack changed wholesale, not just its Docker glue.

  `Suggested:` do **not** merge #1142 or retry it — the Node 22 migration removed the *import-time* blocker (`markAsUncloneable` is absent from post-migration logs and 425/426 tests now pass), but it exposed a second, genuine incompatibility underneath. Either (a) pin `@testcontainers/postgresql` at `^11.x` and add a dependabot `ignore` for majors until this is root-caused, or (b) reproduce locally with `./scripts/spec-loop.sh src/discord-bot/lfg-board/lfg-board-parity.integration.spec.ts 50` on a testcontainers-12 branch per the STRICT flake protocol, and instrument the container's lifecycle around the ECONNRESET before designing a fix.

  **Process note for the next agent:** the wrong call was made from a single failing run by matching the error string against the known `reference_integration_404_socket_carrier` family. The string matched; the causal claim did not follow from it. One failure was enough to justify *a* rerun as a test — it was not enough to justify the conclusion, and the conclusion should not have been written down until the rerun came back.

### 2026-09-11 — feat/rok-1309-cohort-memory (surfaced during ROK-1309 S1, contract DTO slice)

- **[med]** `packages/contract/src/__tests__/` — **no test runner includes this directory, and its specs have rotted red as a result.** The root `vitest.config.ts` `include` is `web/src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}` + `scripts/smoke/**/*.spec.ts`; `packages/contract/package.json` has no `test` script (only `build` + a stubbed `lint`). Running them explicitly (`npx vitest run --root packages/contract`) on a clean `origin/main` checkout gives **6 failed | 42 passed**: `lineup.schema.spec.ts:187/193/199` (and its compiled `dist/` twin) fail with `"Invalid input: expected string, received undefined"` on `nominationTargetDisarmedAt` and `"expected boolean, received undefined"` on `nominationTargetArmed` — `LineupDetailResponseSchema` gained required fields after those specs were written and the `baseLineup()` fixture was never updated. Confirmed pre-existing: the failures are in `LineupDetailResponseSchema`, which this branch does not touch (this branch only adds a new `lineup-cohort-memory.schema.ts` + its own spec, which passes).
  `Suggested:` either add `packages/contract/src/**/*.spec.ts` to the root vitest `include` (and fix the two stale fixtures in the same PR — they are two missing keys), or delete the directory. A spec suite that cannot fail is worse than no spec suite: ROK-1309 S1 shipped its contract test into a directory where CI will never notice it regressing.
- **[nit]** `packages/contract/dist/` is committed-adjacent enough that `vitest --root packages/contract` picks up **both** `src/*.spec.ts` and the compiled `dist/*.spec.js`, double-running every contract spec. Harmless today, but if the include above is fixed, exclude `dist/`.

### 2026-09-12 — feat/rok-1309-cohort-memory (surfaced during ROK-1309 S1 fleet gate)

- **[med]** `scripts/validate-ci.sh --full` **does not fit inside the rl-infra task watchdog when the fleet is contended.** Task `250ccc579409` (`--full --no-e2e --no-coverage`, slot 2) ran Build/TypeScript/Lint/Unit/Tools-unit all `PASS`, then was killed mid-**integration** by `[task-watchdog] timeout (1800s) reached — sending SIGTERM to pgid 348212`. `script_exit_code` is **143 (SIGTERM), not an assertion failure** — the recorded `status: "failed"` is a timeout, and reading it as a red test run is the trap. Two peer `rl_validate_ci` tasks on the same host finished at ~1632s and ~1641s, i.e. *under* the 1800s cap by ~10 seconds, so this is not specific to this branch: with 3–4 heavy tasks admitted concurrently, a `--full` run sits right on the cap and whichever task is unluckiest gets SIGTERMed in integration.
  `Suggested:` either (a) raise the per-task watchdog above 1800s for the `rl_validate_ci` tool specifically, (b) have `rl_validate_ci` surface `143`/watchdog kills as a distinct `timed_out` status so agents stop triaging them as test failures, or (c) split the `--full` gate into two dispatches (static+unit, then integration) so neither half approaches the cap. Until then: a `--full` fleet gate that reports `failed` should have `script_exit_code` checked **first** — `143` plus a `task-watchdog` line in the log tail means "not finished", not "broken".

### 2026-09-12 — feat/rok-1525-player-count-filter (surfaced during the ROK-1525 Playwright smoke slice)

- **med (real product race, reproduced then worked around in the spec)** `web/src/pages/games/use-lfg-filter-param.ts:92-100` and `web/src/pages/games/use-library-filter-params.ts:155-165` — two chip clicks in quick succession can silently drop the FIRST chip's param. Reproduced on the desktop project of `scripts/smoke/library-filters.smoke.spec.ts`: after `?players=5plus` was already in the address bar, clicking `Players are looking` produced `http://localhost:5173/games?lfg=1` — `expect(page).toHaveURL(/[?&]players=5plus(&|$)/)` then failed for the full 10s window (23 polls), so the loss is permanent, not transient. It passed on retry and on mobile, which is what a commit-timing race looks like.

  **Why it is not the "copy the previous params" bug it resembles:** both writers already use the functional `setSearchParams((prev) => …)` form and both copy `prev` rather than constructing a fresh `URLSearchParams`. The problem is one level down — React Router resolves that updater against the params of the render the callback was created in, so a second write that lands before React has committed the first is handed a `prev` that predates it and legitimately writes a patch over stale state. Nothing in either hook can see that from the inside.

  **Not caused by this branch:** the same shape exists in the shipped ROK-1478 `lfg` writer on `origin/main`; ROK-1525 only added a second chip row next to it, which is what made the collision reachable by a single user gesture pair.

  `Suggested:` resolve each patch against `new URLSearchParams(window.location.search)` instead of the updater's `prev` (one line in each of the two `applyLibraryParams` / `applyLfgParam` call sites), or serialize the writes behind a single shared reducer. Either way keep a smoke case that clicks two chips WITHOUT waiting for the first to commit — the current spec deliberately waits on `aria-pressed` to stay deterministic, which means it no longer covers this.

  **RESOLVED on this branch (2026-09-12, commit `e1bf1667`).** `use-search-param-write.ts` now resolves every patch against the params most recently WRITTEN rather than the updater's `prev`, and the smoke case was un-barriered (`6bad3f22`) so it clicks two chips with nothing waiting in between — the coverage this entry asked for. A same-tick double write and back-to-back real clicks both compose to `?players=4&owners=2`. Kept here rather than deleted: the `prev`-resolution shape is still live in any FUTURE search-param writer that does not go through that hook.

- **note (local dev env, not a repo defect — recorded so the next agent does not re-diagnose it)** `./scripts/deploy_dev.sh --ci --rebuild` refused to start with `Migration drift: DB has migration(s) NOT in this branch's journal (orphan rows): hash 768cb8e1f2d6055f…`. The local Postgres carried a migration from another lane's branch (slots 1/3 were running `tech-debt/rok-1101-invitee-migrations` and `feat/rok-1160-restore-drill`). `reconcile-migrations.mjs` cannot remove an orphan row, so the recovery was `deploy_dev.sh --ci --rebuild --fresh`. Matches memory `reference_migration_draft_hash_orphan.md`.

It also passed in the same commit's full fleet run (807 passed / 0 failed, both projects).

### 2026-09-12 — fix/rok-1529-lfg-parity-econnreset (surfaced during ROK-1529 flake investigation)

- **RESOLVED 2026-09-12 (ROK-1529)** — `api/src/discord-bot/lfg-board/lfg-board-parity.integration.spec.ts` failing `connect ECONNRESET 127.0.0.1:<ephemeral>` in ~74 ms was **supertest owning a per-request listener**, not Postgres, Redis, or Testcontainers. `api/src/common/testing/test-app.ts` handed `supertest.default(httpServer)` a server that Nest's `app.init()` never made listen, so supertest took its `if (!addr) this._server = app.listen(0)` branch (`supertest/lib/test.js:63`) and closed that listener the moment its own response ended (`test.js:143`). Sequential specs never noticed. This spec's first `it()` opens with `Promise.all` of four `createMemberAndLogin` logins: the first Test binds and then closes the ephemeral port, while its three siblings — which found `app.address()` already populated and so set no `_server` of their own — are still opening sockets against it, and fail at the CONNECT phase. Fix is one bind in the shared helper (`listenOnEphemeralPort`, called before the agent is constructed), so `app.address()` is non-null for good and supertest never creates or closes a listener; `closeTestApp` → `app.close()` still tears it down. Every integration spec that fans out with `Promise.all` benefits, not just this one. Evidence: baseline **4/20** on untouched `origin/main`, **0/50** with the fix, and **5/20** when the original code is re-applied (`./scripts/spec-loop.sh lfg-board-parity`).
- **Correction to the 2026-09-11 `rok-1526 follow-up` entry (PR #1142 / testcontainers 12), `med`:** that entry reads the same `connect ECONNRESET 127.0.0.1:<high port>` in this same spec as "the suite's connection to its own Postgres container being reset ... exactly the surface testcontainers 12 changed", and recommends pinning `@testcontainers/postgresql` at `^11.x`. That attribution does not hold. The port is supertest's own ephemeral listener, not a Testcontainers-mapped host port, and the flake **reproduces on `origin/main` at testcontainers 11** (4/20 measured above) — so the entry's premise that the spec is a clean `PASS` on `origin/main` rested on a single green observation of a ~20-25% flake. Testcontainers 12 most likely only shifted timing enough to make the pre-existing race land more often. `Suggested:` re-evaluate #1142 on top of this fix before pinning testcontainers to `^11.x` — the pin may be treating a symptom that is now gone.

### 2026-09-12 — feat/rok-1310-cohort-insights (surfaced during the ROK-1309/1310 fleet gate)

- **[med]** `tools/mcp-env/src/tools/env-lock.bash.integration.test.ts:59` — the **"Tools unit tests (mcp servers)"** step of `validate-ci.sh --fleet` failed with `Error: Test timed out in 5000ms.` on `agent_id primary match (ROK-1318) > release succeeds via agent_id when worktree differs from holder.worktree` (1 failed | 107 passed). Because the gate stops on first failure, this **aborted the run before integration and e2e ever started** — the expensive half of the gate was never reached, which is what makes a 5-second timeout in an unrelated workspace costly.
  Confirmed pre-existing and load-induced, not a branch regression: (a) this branch changes **nothing** under `tools/` (`git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD -- tools/` is empty) and the spec is unchanged on `origin/main` since #843 (2026-05-23); (b) re-run in isolation on the same runner immediately afterwards it passed **3/3 at ~1.0s per run** — a 5× margin under the 5000ms cap. The spec shells out to `scripts/env-lock.sh` per assertion, so its wall time is subprocess-spawn-bound; under a contended host (this run was admitted with 2 other heavy tasks already running) the spawn latency alone blows the vitest **default 5s** `testTimeout`.
  `Suggested:` give the bash-shelling specs in `tools/mcp-env` an explicit `testTimeout` (15–20s) — either per-`describe` or via `test.timeout` in `tools/mcp-env/vitest.config.ts` — so host contention stops masquerading as a logic failure. Secondarily, consider whether "Tools unit tests" should be fail-fast ahead of integration in `--fleet` mode at all, given a flake there discards the whole gate.
### 2026-09-12 — fix/fleet-playwright-parity (ROK-1533 — RESOLVES the fleet-path Playwright family)

Supersedes the three earlier entries about this family: **L545** (2026-09-02, rok-1466-playwright-on-fleet),
**L883** (the same three specs re-observed on ROK-1446), and **L1022** (2026-09-06, fix/batch-2026-09-06).
Both hypotheses recorded there are now **DISPROVEN**, and all six specs are fixed on this branch.

- **RESOLVED — `scripts/smoke/community-lineup.smoke.spec.ts:446` + `scripts/smoke/events.smoke.spec.ts:282` (mobile).**
  Not a viewport/DPR breakpoint flip (L545/L883) and not a config-side `storageState` anchor (L1022 —
  `playwright.config.ts` was already correct, which is why the other 756 tests authenticated in the same run).
  Both specs build their OWN context with the literal `storageState: 'scripts/.auth/admin.json'`; ROK-1466 moved
  the auth dir out of the Mutagen-replicated tree on a runner (`scripts/auth-paths.ts::resolveAuthDir` →
  `/tmp/rl-playwright-auth`, `validate-ci.sh` exports `PLAYWRIGHT_AUTH_DIR`), so the literal ENOENTs inside
  `browser.newContext()` in ~40 ms and **not one assertion in either test has ever executed on the fleet**.
  They were the only two bypasses of `STORAGE_STATE_PATH` in `scripts/**`. Deterministic, 2/2 on every observed
  fleet run. Fixed by importing the shared constant.
- **RESOLVED — `scripts/smoke/lineup-auto-advance.smoke.spec.ts:129` (desktop AND mobile).** Not websocket latency
  through the edge (L545) — the socket never connects at all, on ANY nginx-fronted deployment **including
  production**. `VITE_API_URL` is an origin locally but the path `/api` in every built image, and
  socket.io-client resolves a leading-slash uri against `location` then uses the resolved path as the
  NAMESPACE, so the bundle asked for `/api/lineups` (the gateway registers `/lineups`) over `/socket.io/`,
  which nginx answers with `index.html`. Protocol probe against a live env: `io("/api/lineups")` →
  `CONNECT_ERROR: server error`; `io("/lineups", { path: "/api/socket.io" })` → `CONNECTED nsp=/lineups`.
  Product bug, fixed in `web/src/lib/socket-target.ts` (+ unit test); `use-voice-roster.ts` had the identical
  defect on `/ad-hoc`. An nginx-only `/socket.io` proxy would NOT have helped — the namespace stays wrong.
- **RESOLVED — `scripts/smoke/lineup-tie-readiness.smoke.spec.ts:168` + `scripts/smoke/lineup-tiebreaker.smoke.spec.ts:643`.**
  Nothing to do with realtime — neither page mounts `useLineupRealtime`. Both render from the GLOBAL
  `GET /lineups/banner` singleton (`api/src/lineups/lineups-banner.helpers.ts::findBannerLineup` is
  `orderBy(desc(createdAt)).limit(1)`, no per-lineup scoping), so on the fleet — one env serving desktop +
  mobile + every other lane — a sibling spec's newer lineup owns the banner and the copy under test is not on
  the page (confirmed by a DOM snapshot naming another spec's lineup as the owner). GitHub CI shards to five
  envs, hence fleet-only. Fixed with `waitForBannerOwnership` (`scripts/smoke/api-helpers.ts`), which also names
  the thief instead of timing out on a selector. **Falsified:** `--workers=1` left them failing 5/10 and 4/10,
  so serialising the run is not the fix.

Still open, filed as follow-ups rather than fixed here:

- **med (test-fixture architecture)** The whole lineup smoke family shares ONE global active lineup by
  construction (`POST /lineups` 409s while one is active) and `ensureVotingLineup` adopts whatever
  `/lineups/banner` returns, **including a lineup another spec file owns**. `waitForBannerOwnership` removes the
  self-inflicted ordering race but cannot stop a foreign lineup created in the ~1 s between the ownership check
  and the page load. `Suggested:` give the banner endpoint optional per-lineup scoping for tests, or give each
  Playwright project its own fleet env.
- **low (unclosed)** `scripts/smoke/community-lineup.smoke.spec.ts:575` (leaderboard sorted by votes) reproduced
  only in a full fleet suite, never in 30 targeted runs across four configurations, and passed under both
  parallel and `--workers=1`. Narrowed to the shared-lineup mechanism above (the lineup is flipped out of
  `voting` between the block's one-shot `beforeAll` and the navigation, so `voting-leaderboard-v2` never mounts),
  not to worker concurrency. `Suggested:` on the next full-suite hit, read
  `test-results/community-lineup.smoke-Vot-*/error-context.md` to see which phase surface rendered; if Decided,
  add `cancelLineupPhaseJobs` after the advance (a BullMQ auto-advance job firing on schedule explains why
  `--workers=1` changed nothing).
- **med (fleet tooling; credential hygiene — re-flagging L1022's sibling)** `rl_task_status` / `rl_task_inspect`
  still echo `ADMIN_PASSWORD=<value>` in cleartext in the task `cmd` array for every `rl_validate_ci … --only-e2e`
  dispatch. Any agent polling a task sees it. `Suggested:` redact `*_PASSWORD=` / `*_TOKEN=` assignments at the
  MCP boundary, as PR #1091 already does for `rl_env_deploy`'s `admin_password`.

### 2026-09-12 — fix/fleet-playwright-parity (surfaced during the ROK-1533 fleet gate; report-only)

- **med (fleet harness; pre-existing — NOT this branch)** `rl_validate_ci --only-e2e --with-e2e` from a FRESH worktree fails the Discord-smoke step with `Error: Missing required env var: TEST_BOT_TOKEN` at `tools/test-bot/src/config.ts:10`, after Playwright has already PASSed. Cause: `tools/test-bot/.env` is gitignored, so a newly created worktree does not have it and Mutagen replicates a tree without it — the main repo's copy is never seen by the runner. Consequence is worse than one red step: `validate-ci.sh` stops on first failure, so the run exits 1, `playwright_verified` stays **false** and **no sentinel is written**, which means a branch whose Playwright tier is genuinely green still cannot satisfy the pre-push sentinel gate. Confirmed on task `ee580cf38f66` (Playwright PASS 795/0, Discord smoke FAIL on the missing token). Also note `--with-e2e` force-runs Discord smoke even for a diff that touches no `api/src/discord-bot/**`, `api/src/notifications/**` or `tools/test-bot/**` path — plain `--only-e2e` correctly scopes it out. `Suggested:` have `scripts/deploy_dev.sh`-style env propagation cover worktrees for `tools/test-bot/.env` (the deploy script already copies `.env` + `api/.env`), or have `validate-ci.sh` SKIP Discord smoke with an explicit reason when `TEST_BOT_TOKEN` is absent instead of hard-failing — a missing local credential is not a code regression, and today it silently blocks the sentinel.
- **low (shared-env Playwright flakes; pre-existing)** Same task, 2 flaky (both recovered on retry, both outside this branch's six): `scripts/smoke/community-lineup.smoke.spec.ts:234` (nomination-modal search) and `:486` (banner visible on mobile viewport). Both are the documented shared-fleet-env family — one env serving desktop + mobile + every other lane — and `:486` is banner-singleton contention of exactly the kind ROK-1533 fixed for `:446`. `Suggested:` fold `:486` into the `claimBannerOwnership` pattern the tie-readiness/tiebreaker specs now use.

### 2026-09-12 — feat/rok-1310-cohort-insights (surfaced during the ROK-1309/1310 pre-push gate)

- **[high]** `scripts/playwright-global-setup.ts:52,103,130,155` — the Playwright tier of `validate-ci.sh --fleet` failed with `TypeError: fetch failed` / `[cause]: ConnectTimeoutError: Connect Timeout Error (attempted addresses: 172.67.175.107:443, timeout: 10000ms)` in **global setup, before a single test ran** (task `f0997cf784d6`; every other step PASS, including integration 45 suites / 437 tests, 4/4 shards). `172.67.175.107` is one of the two Cloudflare A-records fronting `*.gamernight.net`, so this is the same undici 10s connect-timeout class already recorded for `scripts/smoke/api-helpers.ts`. PR #1188 added `scripts/smoke/fetch-retry.ts` and wired it into `api-helpers.ts`, but **global setup still calls bare `fetch` at all four call sites** and is therefore uncovered. The blast radius is larger there than in any spec: global setup is a hard prerequisite, so one dropped connect fails the ENTIRE e2e tier, `playwright_verified` stays false and **no pre-push sentinel is written** — a branch whose tests are all green cannot pass the sentinel gate. Not branch-caused: this branch touches no file under `scripts/` (`git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD -- scripts/` is empty), and a re-dispatch of `--only-e2e` against the same env cleared it.
  `Suggested:` wrap the four `fetch` calls in `scripts/playwright-global-setup.ts` with the existing `fetchWithRetry` from `scripts/smoke/fetch-retry.ts` (transport/connect errors only, never on a 4xx/5xx response) — the helper and its spec already exist on main, this is purely wiring the last uncovered caller.

### 2026-09-13 — feat/rok-1538-cohort-common-ground-row (surfaced during the ROK-1538 phase-C fleet gate; report-only)

- **med (shared-env Playwright flakes; pre-existing — NOT this branch)** Across three consecutive Playwright tiers on ONE long-lived fleet env (`rok1538`, slot 1), the failing set changed completely on every run while the code under test did not — the shared-env contention signature, not a regression. Run `c10387116f37` (full gate): `scripts/smoke/lfg-group-page.smoke.spec.ts:255` + `scripts/smoke/lineup-tiebreaker.smoke.spec.ts` (bracket matchup, mobile). Run `cde33afc48ed`: `scripts/smoke/community-lineup.smoke.spec.ts:234`. Run `9577d8e83d70`: `scripts/smoke/games.smoke.spec.ts:180` and `:189` (co-op FilterPanel, ROK-1402), `scripts/smoke/lfg-chips.smoke.spec.ts:240`, `scripts/smoke/lineup-tie-readiness.smoke.spec.ts:124` (desktop **and** mobile) — while `lfg-group-page:255` and `community-lineup:220`, which had FAILED in an earlier run, recovered as **flaky** (passed on retry) in this one. None of these specs is touched by this branch (`git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` covers only `packages/contract`, `api/src/lineups`, `api/src/drizzle`, `web/src/components/lineups`, `web/src/hooks`, `web/src/lib`, `web/src/test`, and `scripts/smoke/lineup-cohort-row.smoke.spec.ts`). Decisive evidence they are not branch-caused: `games.smoke.spec.ts` and `lfg-chips` PASSED in run `c10387116f37` against the *same* branch code on the *same* env — a real regression would have failed there too. **Consequence is the expensive part:** `validate-ci.sh` stops on first failure, so `playwright_verified` stays false and **no pre-push sentinel is written**, which means a branch whose own six specs are all green (verified: all 3 cohort-row tests × desktop + mobile PASS in `9577d8e83d70`) still cannot satisfy the pre-push gate. This is the same sentinel-blocking shape already recorded on 2026-09-12 for `TEST_BOT_TOKEN` and for the global-setup connect timeout. `Suggested:` two independent levers — (a) have the e2e tier distinguish "failures inside the diff's own specs" from "failures elsewhere" when deciding whether to write the sentinel, or at minimum report the two counts separately so an agent can act on its own failures without re-running the world; and (b) spin a FRESH env for the gate rather than re-using one that has already been mutated by prior full Playwright runs — accumulated cross-spec DB state is the mechanism (it is what produced this branch's own two genuine failures: a creator-only `{admin}` cohort hash that other admin-decided smoke specs also write under).
- **low (smoke fixture; pre-existing — surfaced by this branch, fixed here)** The demo game catalogue contains name pairs where one is a strict prefix of another (`7 Days to Die` / `7 Days to Die 2-Pack`). Any smoke spec that builds a locator from an unanchored game-name regex against the `Nominate ${gameName}` aria-label will hit a strict-mode violation as soon as both games appear in the same container. Fixed in `scripts/smoke/lineup-cohort-row.smoke.spec.ts` by anchoring to `^nominate <name>$`. `Suggested:` grep the other smoke specs for unanchored `new RegExp(\`nominate ${...}\`)` / `getByRole('button', { name: <bare game name> })` patterns and anchor them before they bite the same way.

### 2026-09-17 — fix/poll-expiry-batch-0917 (surfaced during the ROK-1610 integration spec)

- **[med]** `api/src/drizzle/schema/community-lineup-matches.ts:126` — `proposed_time` is `timestamp` (no time zone), so postgres-js writes it as UTC and reads it back in the PROCESS's local zone: on the fleet runner (UTC−6) a slot suggested for `2099-10-10T21:00:00Z` comes back as `2099-10-11T03:00:00Z`, and the event a lock-in creates carries the shifted instant. Invisible in prod only because those containers run UTC; any deploy with a non-UTC `TZ` shifts every poll slot by the offset. Events dodge this because `events.time_range` is a `tsrange` whose `fromDriver` appends `Z` (`api/src/drizzle/schema/events.ts:33-40`). NOT caused by this branch — the ROK-1610 spec was the first assertion to compare a slot instant end-to-end (it now compares against the API's own reading). Suggested: make the column `timestamp with time zone` (migration + backfill), or give it the same UTC-normalising custom type as `tsrange`, and add an integration assertion that runs under a non-UTC `TZ`. Confirmed 2026-09-17 on a UTC−6 runner: the poll page's slot list, its `lockedInTime`, and the event a lock-in creates re-serialise that column three different ways, so off-UTC the page shows a locked-in time that disagrees with its own event.

Pre-existing: both classes shipped with ROK-1479's urgency picker and are untouched by this branch — ROK-1616 reuses the same ramp for the new `tonight` chip, which is how it surfaced. Not a build/lint/test failure, so nothing is red; it is a two-of-fifteen-themes visual inconsistency. `Suggested:` add `/20` and `/30` override rows next to the existing `/10` row in the light-scheme block, then check the picker in `default-light` against `default-dark`.

### 2026-09-18 — fix/rok-1614-plus-one-inherits-horizon (surfaced during the AC4 web audit)

- **[med]** `web/src/pages/game-detail/lfg-toggle-button.tsx:37` — the game-detail "Looking for group" toggle calls `createIntent({ gameId })` with **no urgency**, so it always raises a **week** hand even when the group has live `now` hands. This is the same defect class ROK-1614 just fixed on the Discord board `+1` (a silent week default that keeps a now-group below `LFG_NOW_SPAWN_THRESHOLD` so no session spawns), on a surface ROK-1614's AC4 does not name — AC4 names `LfgJoinControl.tsx`, which always asks the user and therefore cannot default silently. NOT fixed here because the honest fix needs a **contract change**: `LfgGroupSummarySchema` exposes `nowCount` and `soonestNowExpiresAt` but no group horizon, so a client cannot tell a `tonight` group from a `week` one and cannot inherit what it cannot see. Inferring from `nowCount > 0` would handle `now` and silently mis-handle `tonight`. `Suggested:` add a server-computed `horizon: LfgUrgency` to `LfgGroupSummarySchema` (the value `readGroupHorizon` already resolves), then have the toggle pass `{ gameId, urgency: group.horizon }` — which also gives every other read surface the horizon it currently re-derives from `nowCount`. Worth confirming with the operator whether the game-detail toggle should inherit at all, or keep meaning "I'm up for this some time".

### 2026-09-18 — CI failure sweep, 2026-09-04 → 2026-09-18 (operator asked for chronic-failure analysis)

Method: all 30 failed workflow runs in the window, failing job names per run, then failing spec/test names from each run's logs. Counted by DISTINCT DAYS, not raw log lines — retries log each failure ~3×, which inflates a naive count by 3–15×.

- **[low — measurement note, not a defect]** The Playwright tier is **NOT chronically flaky**, contrary to what a raw count suggests. Raw lines rank `scheduling-poll.smoke.spec.ts` at 47 "failures"; by distinct day it is **1**. A single run (`35139735096`, `feat/rok-1584-hero-phone-round`, 2026-09-16) failed **72 distinct specs** at once and inflated every spec's count — that was the tablet-project/WebKit collapse (CI installs Chromium only), already fixed on that branch. Excluding it, every other CI run in the window failed 1–3 specs, and only ONE main-branch CI smoke failure occurred before 09-18. `Suggested:` when triaging Playwright, count distinct runs/days and check for a mass-failure outlier before concluding a spec is flaky — memory `feedback_playwright_local_full_run_load_flakes` already says >20 spread failures means environment, not product.
- **[med — chronic, UNDOCUMENTED until now]** `tools/test-bot/src/smoke/tests/*` — **"ROK-NNNN: scheduling poll embed has no components and a Vote now link"** failed on **three unrelated branches plus main** (2026-09-14 `feat/rok-1499-room-recap`, 2026-09-14 `main`, 2026-09-15 `fix/rok-1559-heatmap-day-offset`). Cross-branch spread is what distinguishes it from one branch's own iteration. It appears in NEITHER this backlog (0 hits for "Vote now link") NOR `reference_known_smoke_flakes`, so every encounter costs a fresh investigation. `Suggested:` reproduce with the companion-bot loop, then either fix or add it to `reference_known_smoke_flakes` with its signature so the next agent reruns instead of investigating.
- **[low — mostly one branch, but undocumented]** **"LFG board: every hand posts — one forum thread per group, edited in place through conversion"** — 6 occurrences, but **5 of 6 on `feat/rok-1541-lfg-thread-members`**, a branch whose whole subject was LFG board threads, so those read as that story's own iteration rather than a standing flake. The remaining one is on **main, 2026-09-15**. Undocumented in both the backlog and the known-flakes memory. `Suggested:` leave it unless it recurs on main; if it does, it belongs in `reference_known_smoke_flakes`.
- **[note — already documented, confirmed still live]** "Event embed chrome: per-state colour + author line" (this file, 2026-09-05 entry, `Expected embed color #f59e0b, got #38bdf8`) recurred on 3 branches in this window including main 2026-09-18 — the existing entry is accurate and still worth acting on. Same for "Voice classification populates attendance and metrics (ROK-943)" (2026-09-05 entry).
- **[note]** `discord-smoke` is the genuinely recurring tier: **14 of 30 failures**, spread across every single day in the window (09-14 … 09-18), versus Playwright's 3 days. If one tier deserves investment, it is this one.
- ~~**[CORRECTION to the line above, same sweep]** An earlier version of this entry blamed the **workflow-level** `discord-smoke-shared-guild` concurrency group. **That is stale and wrong** — the group was moved to JOB level on 2026-09-08 (`745c1a5e` / PR #1121) and `.github/workflows/discord-smoke.yml:64` confirms it is job-scoped today. ROK-1522 flagged it, and it was **struck in place by PR #1258** (search `RESOLVED 2026-09-08 — WAS med`). Recorded here because the wrong version of this claim had already been re-derived once during this very sweep — that is what the strike exists to stop. The residual contention problem — GitHub holding one pending run per group and cancelling the queued one — is real but is ALREADY TRACKED as ROK-1522, and is a contention/eviction problem, not a cause of the assertion failures counted above.~~ — **struck 2026-09-23 (ROK-1620): concurrency is job-level since PR #1121.**

### 2026-09-19 — feat/rok-1613-start-now-web (surfaced while gating the ROK-1613 web button)

- **[med]** `web/tsconfig.app.json:28` — the web typecheck EXCLUDES every spec
  file: `"exclude": ["src/**/*.test.ts", "src/**/*.test.tsx", "src/test/**"]`.
  So `npm run build -w web` (and therefore the `--static` fleet gate's
  "TypeScript (all)" step) never typechecks `*.test.tsx` or the shared test
  helpers. Vitest strips types with esbuild rather than checking them, so a
  type error in a spec — most commonly a required prop added to a shared
  component, which breaks every existing construction of its props — passes
  both the gate and the test run, and surfaces only if someone runs `tsc` by
  hand. This is the web twin of the already-documented
  `npm run build -w api` gap (the API gate needs an explicit
  `npx tsc --noEmit -p api/tsconfig.json`), but the web side has no equivalent
  second command anywhere in the pipeline.
  Verified on this branch: adding a required `onStartNow` prop to `LfgHeroProps`
  left `LfgHero.test.tsx` uncompilable while `npm run build -w web` stayed
  green.
  Suggested: add a `typecheck:tests` script running `tsc --noEmit` over a config
  that includes `src/**` with `types: ["vite/client", "vitest/globals", "node"]`,
  and wire it into `validate-ci.sh --static`. NOTE: a trial run of exactly that
  config reported ~266 pre-existing errors across the existing specs (largely
  missing `jest-axe` matcher types for `toHaveNoViolations`, plus some factory
  drift), so this cannot simply be switched on — the backlog has to be cleared
  or the rule introduced per-directory first. Sizing that cleanup is the real
  task here, not the config change.

- **[ROOT CAUSE for the `standalone-scheduling-poll.smoke.spec.ts:494` sightings above, and for
  `scheduling-poll-live-updates.smoke.spec.ts:216`]** On a phone layout (<1024px) the
  "confirm your game time" sheet UNMOUNTS the slot list —
  `web/src/components/lineups/cycle-4/SchedulingComposite.tsx:217`
  `{!check.sheetVisible && <SchedulingSlotList {...ladder} />}` — and the sheet opens whenever the
  viewer's `game_time_confirmed_at` is NULL, i.e. on every fresh CI database until some OTHER spec
  happens to PUT/PATCH game time. So any phone-layout spec that reads a slot row passes or fails by
  SHARD COMPOSITION, not by project or branch (GitHub run 35434053903: the new anti-vote spec was
  12/12 red on `[mobile]` in shard 4/5 and green on the fleet, whose env DB was already stamped).
  Playwright's `element(s) not found` (not "not visible") is the signature. Fixed in this branch for
  the two specs that did not already call `dismissGameTimeCheck`: a file-level `beforeAll` that
  `PATCH /users/me/game-time/confirm`s. `dismissGameTimeCheck`'s 3 s probe (`scripts/smoke/helpers.ts:67`)
  can still lose the race to the game-time query — that is the `:216` flake.
  Suggested: confirm game time once in Playwright's global setup, and delete the per-spec dismissals.
- **[nit]** `scripts/**` is typechecked by no CI job (found by the ROK-1617 smoke lane): a smoke spec
  with a type error only fails when Playwright loads it. Suggested: a `tsc --noEmit` over
  `scripts/smoke` in `validate-ci.sh --static`.

  asked lane 2 to confirm the OPEN-poll lock-in path enforces creator-or-operator server-side. It does
  NOT: `assertMayLockInSlot` branches on `pollStatus === 'open'` and only runs
  `assertUserHasVoted(db, matchId, caller.id)`, so ANY member who voted may end an open poll;
  `assertCallerMayLockIn` (the organiser gate) runs on the EXPIRED branch only. This is deliberate and
  commented as such ("unchanged behaviour, any member"), and it is pre-existing (ROK-1610), so it is
  recorded rather than changed. Consequence worth a reviewer's eye: the new `/rally` route is
  STRICTER on an open poll than the Lock item sitting next to it in the same ⋯ menu — a plain member
  who voted can lock the poll in but cannot rally it. Suggested: an operator ruling on whether an
  open-poll lock-in should also be organiser-only; if yes, it is a one-line move of
  `assertCallerMayLockIn` above the `pollStatus === 'open'` branch plus a `scheduling-lock-in` spec case.

### 2026-09-19 — perf/rok-1407-stable-registry (surfaced during ROK-1407)

  api-only PR. Suggested: delete the hook call and its two test mocks in one commit — it removes a
