### 2026-10-04 — fix/r2-ci-dashboard-release-1004 (surfaced during TDB:1676 release-path fix + dashboard CI wiring)

- **[resolved 2026-10-04, unproven until the first real dispatched tag]** TDB:1676 (two workflows create the GitHub release for the same tag, both named `Release`). Corrects that entry, which is left as written: its suggested direction was reversed. `.github/workflows/release.yml` no longer creates a release, bumps a version or pushes `main`. It derives the next version from the highest `vX.Y.Z` tag (`scripts/next-release-version.sh`), pushes only an annotated tag, and dispatches `.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml`, renamed `Publish images`, with `gh workflow run --ref vX.Y.Z`. That workflow's `softprops/action-gh-release` step (now passed `tag_name` explicitly) is the only release creator. A `GITHUB_TOKEN` tag push fires no push-event workflow, which is why the dispatch is needed and why the same tag cannot be built twice. `actionlint` is clean on both files, but no workflow test can exercise a real tag push or dispatch, so the path is proven only when the first real release is cut. Suggested: on the first cut, confirm one `Release` run, one `Publish images` run on the tag, the `X.Y.Z` / `X.Y` / sha image tags in GHCR, and a single GitHub release with the Quick Deploy body followed by generated notes.
- **[resolved 2026-10-04 — moot]** Latent: lockfile drift from the old `release.yml:86` bump commit. `npm version -w api` rewrote `api/package.json` but the commit staged only that file, so `package-lock.json` would have drifted on every cut. There is no bump commit any more, so nothing can drift. (The separate `api/package.json` version sync is unaffected and still open.)
- **[resolved 2026-10-04 — moot]** Latent: the old `release.yml` had no guard that the bumped version is greater than the latest `v*` tag. It bumped from `api/package.json`, which lags the tags, so a cut could have produced a version below or equal to an existing tag. The version is now derived from the global highest existing `vX.Y.Z` tag, so the bump is greater than every release tag; the script's exit-1 existing-tag check is only a defensive backstop.
- **[nit]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:584`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:745` — actionlint/shellcheck `SC2034: i appears unused. Verify use (or export if used externally).` on retry loops that never read their counter. `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1071` — `SC2126: Consider using 'grep -c' instead of 'grep|wc -l'.` Line numbers are as on `origin/main`, where `actionlint .github/workflows/ci.yml` reports all three; this branch adds an unrelated hunk above them, so on the branch they sit at `:592`, `:753` and `:1079`. Suggested: rename the loop counters to `_` and use `grep -c`.
### 2026-10-04 — fix/b56-orchestrator-tests-1004 (resolutions and review follow-ups)

- **[resolved 2026-10-04]** The 2026-09-07 fix/batch-2026-09-07 entry for `rl-infra/orchestrator/test/run-on-runner-exec-bits.test.sh::X1 / X3` (`fixture precondition: the canary must be 0644 going in`). The recorded cause was wrong: Mutagen's `restore-exec-bits` pass never touched the canary, and moving it out of `/workspace` would not have helped. The specs probed BSD `stat -f '%Lp'` first. On the GNU runner, `stat -f` means `--file-system` and prints filesystem stats with exit 0, so the probe captured that junk plus the real mode and the precondition compare failed. `run-on-runner-exec-bits.test.sh` (`_mode_of`, `_probe_cmd`) and `runner-exec-bits.test.sh` (`mode_of`) now try GNU `stat -c '%a'` first, the same shape as `rl-infra/runner/ensure-runner-dirs.sh`. Runner, slot 1: 8 pass / 3 fail before, 11/0 after; `runner-exec-bits` 18/0 both times.
- **[fix landed, runner verification PENDING — do not strike yet]** The 2026-09-07 fix/batch-2026-09-07 entry for `rl-infra/orchestrator/test/release-preserve-envs.test.sh::TOCTOU …` (`extraction failed`). Reproduced 1 of 3 runs on a runner (`sed: can't read /workspace/rl-infra/orchestrator/bin/lease-advance: No such file or directory`). The sed range was not the cause. The STRICT task-cancel-cascade case moved the REAL `bin/task-cancel` and `bin/lease-advance` aside, symlinked fakes in, then moved them back. On a runner `/workspace` is a live Mutagen-synced tree, and the runner's `lease-advance` mtime jumped during the run, which `mv` alone would not do. That points to a mid-suite Mutagen rewrite racing the TOCTOU case's extraction, but this is inferred from the mtime, not observed directly. The STRICT case now runs `release` from a `cp -p` copy of `bin/` with the two fakes swapped in, so the real `bin/` is never modified (laptop 19/0, `ls -li bin/` unchanged). Still needed before this is struck: at least 3 runs on a runner synced to the branch with 0 failures and an unchanged `bin/lease-advance` mtime. If it still fails there, the cause is elsewhere and this item stays open.
- **[low]** `rl-infra/orchestrator/test/release-runner-cleanup.test.sh:91-113` (`_link_stubs_into_bin_dir` / `_restore_stubs`): same anti-pattern as above. It moves `task-cancel`, `lease-advance` and `env-destroy` out of the real `$BIN_DIR` and symlinks fakes in. The file is not in `run-tests.sh`'s default list, so the default suite is unaffected. Run on its own on a runner, though, it has the same exposure to Mutagen rewrites, and on a two-way sync it could change the laptop's files.
  Suggested: run `release` from a `cp -p` copy of `bin/` with the fakes swapped in, as `release-preserve-envs.test.sh`'s STRICT case now does.
- **[nit]** `rl-infra/orchestrator/test/extra-slots-provisioning.test.sh:69`: still tries BSD `stat -f '%p'` before `stat -c`. It works, because each fallback reassigns `m` and drops the failed probe's output, but it does not follow the GNU-first order the exec-bits specs now use.
  Suggested: switch it to GNU-first in a later chore so every stat probe in the suite reads the same way.
### 2026-10-04 — fix/r3-rlinfra-sweeper-bundle-1004 (review nit left open)

- **[nit]** `rl-infra/gc-sweeper/sweep.sh` (dead-claim reaper §1b and the expired-claim reaper §1d): a slot these reapers free is not handed to a queued waiter promptly. `claim-wait` wakes only on a change to `$RL_LEASE_QUEUE_DIR` (inotifywait close_write/moved_to/delete), and the reapers write `claims.json`, not the queue dir, so the waiter gets the slot only at its next unrelated queue wake or when its wait times out. §1d is hit hardest: it releases only when a waiter exists, and that waiter then sits out its timeout. Behaviour is unchanged from main (the old in-container `lease-advance` call never ran).
  Suggested: after a reap frees a slot that has waiters, touch a file in that slot's lease-queue dir so `claim-wait` wakes and re-runs `claim`.
### 2026-10-04 — fix/r1-lineups-rulings-1004 (review nits left open)

- **[med]** `api/src/lineups/scheduling/scheduling.controller.ts:293-323`: the private-poll read gate (`assertCallerMayViewPoll`, TDB:189) covers only the poll page read. The sibling reads `GET /lineups/:lineupId/schedule/:matchId/availability` and `/other-polls` are ungated, and `getMatchAvailability` ignores `lineupId` entirely, so any signed-in user can read a private lineup match's availability aggregate. Out of scope for the GET-half ruling on this branch.
  Suggested: apply `assertCallerMayViewPoll` to both reads (resolving the lineup from the match for availability) and add a non-participant 404 case per route.
- **[nit]** `api/src/lineups/lineup-deadline-transition-notify.integration.spec.ts` (AC4 stale-job test): still waits with a fixed `setTimeout(250)` before asserting the hooks did NOT fire. Pre-existing; the AC5 case on this branch now waits deterministically.
  Suggested: assert on the synchronous state the early `status !== expectedFrom` return leaves (no activity row, no `scheduleTransition` call) and drop the sleep.

### 2026-10-05 — fix/lineup-submit-tz-test (surfaced fixing the lineup-submit AC5 timezone failure)

- **med (latent, prod)** Naive-timestamp timezone coupling. `Dockerfile.allinone` sets no `TZ` and installs no tzdata, so Node and Postgres both default to UTC today and nothing is skewed. If the NAS container is ever given a non-UTC `TZ` (or Postgres `timezone` was initialised non-UTC), values diverge between SQL `now()` and JS-written Dates on the 148 `timestamp` columns WITHOUT time zone (16 have one): e.g. `api/src/lineups/submit/lineup-submit.service.ts:111-114`, `api/src/lineups/scheduling/scheduling-submitted-at.helpers.ts:50`, `voter-scope.helpers.ts:85`, every `defaultNow()` on a naive column; and `api/src/taste-profile/queries/taste-profile-queries.ts:184` parses `last_played_at` with a bare `new Date()` (shifts when Node TZ is not UTC). Found by the lineup-submit AC5 fix (fleet runner has `TZ=America/Denver`). Suggested: operator runs `SHOW timezone` on prod; pin `TZ=UTC` in the allinone image and the DB connection to UTC; switch `:184` to `parseTimestampUtc` (`api/src/drizzle/timestamp-utils.ts`).

### 2026-10-08 — fix/b71-r6-contract-prune (surfaced during the B71 `--full` gate, task 4f729635dad3)

- **[low]** `api/src/discord-bot/lfm/lfm-embed.integration.spec.ts:313` "reconcile provenance lookups (D9) › resolvePollTarget turns the match id into the /schedule/:matchId link parts" — `expect(second.id).not.toBe(first.lineupId)  Expected: not 22`. The assertion compares a match id to a lineup id; they come from independent serial sequences and coincide whenever both tables have handed out the same count (seen at 22). Pre-existing: B71 doesn't touch `lfm/`; an `only_integration` rerun at the same HEAD passed (fa2fc8c32058). Suggested: assert the link uses the match id (`toBe(second.id)` on the produced path) instead of asserting the two ids differ, or seed the match so its id is provably distinct.
### 2026-10-02 — light raw-hue repaints (surfaced while adding the WoW item tooltip's text-gray-400 keep-dark rule)
### 2026-10-02 — light raw-hue repaints branch (review NIT deferred)

- **[nit]** `web/src/pages/onboarding-wizard-page.tsx:243-247` (`WizardHeader` Skip All): a raw `<button>` with hand-rolled `disabled` / `aria-busy` / "Skipping…" pending copy. The shared `Button` (`web/src/components/ui/button.tsx:102-121`) maps `loading` to `aria-busy` + `aria-disabled` and keeps focus on click, where native `disabled` drops focus to `body` mid-click. Not migrated with the pending-state fix: `loading` hides the visible label behind a spinner (the "Skipping…" copy survives only as the sr-only name), and `onboarding-wizard-page.invite-claim.test.tsx` pins native `toBeDisabled()`, so the swap changes the visible state and the assertion together.
  Suggested: move to `<Button variant="ghost" loading={isPending} loadingLabel="Skipping…">`, assert `aria-disabled` plus the accessible name in that spec, and update `form-primitives.baseline.json` if the file's raw-button count changes.

### 2026-10-08 — fix/theme-2052-dark-badge-label (surfaced during theme Lane B review)

- **[low]** `web/src/pages/calendar/CalendarGameFilter.tsx:189` — the selected-game CheckMark puts `bg-success` on the box and `text-white` on the child `<svg>`, so the status-label rule (same-element `.bg-success.text-white` → `--color-status-solid-label`) does not reach it: the white check is 2.54:1 on dark `#10b981`, below the 3:1 non-text floor. Pre-existing (not a `.text-white` label on the fill element; untouched by TDB:2052).
  Suggested: move `text-white` onto the `bg-success` box so the label token paints the check (`currentColor`), or use a dark check icon on dark.
- **[low]** `web/src/components/admin/UserManagementRow.tsx:78` (`BANNED_TONE` `bg-danger/15 text-danger`) and `web/src/components/ui/button.tsx:43` (`destructive-soft` `hover:bg-danger/20`) — dark `text-danger` `#f87171` is 4.26 (default-dark) / 4.36 (forest) / 4.49 (ember) on the `/15` tint over the panel and 3.94 on the `/20` hover over the default-dark panel (Lane B review). Below 4.5 but up from 3.40 / 3.22 under `#ef4444`; `semantic-tokens.guard.test.ts` measures `/10` only. Not a TDB:1770 regression.
  Suggested: the 1770 slices move semantic tints to `/10` — drop `BANNED_TONE` to `/10` in the admin slice, and either accept the transient `/20` hover or extend the guard to the alphas actually shipped.
- **[med — pre-existing, NOT this branch]** `scripts/smoke/lineup-creation.smoke.spec.ts:337` ("Phase countdown display › detail page shows full countdown timer"): `[desktop]` failed on the first run AND Retry #1 in fleet task `ca198fd05a7a` (slot 3, `fix/theme-2052-dark-badge-label` @ `8d0db2c6a`). `[mobile]` was flaky and `[tablet]` passed. Error: `Error: expect(locator).toBeVisible() failed / Locator: getByText(/remaining|countdown|time left/i) / Expected: visible / Timeout: 10000ms / Error: element(s) not found`. This corrects TDB:2204, which blamed the lineup race. The detail page has rendered no countdown since ROK-1323 (PR #871, `b73c0db9c`) removed `<PhaseCountdown … />` from `LineupDetailHeader`. `PhaseCountdown` is now used only by `LineupBanner` on `/games` (`LineupBanner.tsx:47`), and `FullCountdown` has no caller. The test passes only when react-router 7's `startTransition` navigation keeps the `/games` banner's "… remaining" on screen while the lazy `LineupDetailPage` chunk loads, which means it asserts on the page it just left. That makes it a timing race on every branch. The branch diff is colour tokens only, and "not found" (no matching DOM node) is not something CSS can cause. Suggested: wait for a detail-page anchor (a `LineupDetailHeader` testid) before asserting, then assert on what the detail page actually shows for a building lineup. If product still wants a deadline on the detail page, restore one there and delete the dead `FullCountdown`.
